## 1. [Beyond the Inner Slope $\gamma$: A Schwarzschild-based Probe of the Core-Cusp Problem in the Milky Way Dwarf Spheroidal Galaxies](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.17835) <a id="link1"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2611.17835

**Authors:** Rin Miyazaki, Masashi Chiba, Shunichi Horigome

**Abstract:** We present a spherical Schwarzschild orbit-superposition framework for constraining the dark matter density profiles and stellar orbital structures of the Milky Way dwarf spheroidal galaxies. We model the line-of-sight velocity distribution with a Gaussian mixture whose parameters vary smoothly with projected radius through a mixture density network trained on unbinned stellar velocities. This approach reduces the effect of statistical fluctuations inherent in velocity moments on their kinematic constraints. Their covariance and smoothing-induced correlations are included, and the halo parameters are sampled with Markov Chain Monte Carlo. Mock tests for this model show that the density profiles and slopes near the half-light radius are generally recovered well, although the inferred velocity anisotropy shows some systematic deviations. Applying the model to Sculptor, Draco, and Ursa Minor, we find that the enclosed mass $M_h$ and logarithmic density slope $S_h$ at the projected half-light radius are less sensitive to the adopted two different priors than the asymptotic inner slope $\gamma$, consistent with earlier studies. We further compare the inferred slopes at the three-dimensional half-light radius $r_h$ and $0.5r_h$ for a given stellar mass with FIRE-2 $\Lambda$ cold dark matter ($\Lambda$CDM) and self-interacting dark matter (SIDM) cosmological simulations including baryonic feedback. This comparison avoids introducing halo masses or virial radii that are not directly observable. The inferred slopes are broadly consistent with FIRE-2 $\Lambda$CDM predictions, although current uncertainties and the limited SIDM sample do not yet allow a robust distinction between dark matter models.

**Comment:** Core-cusp problem in Milky Way dwarf spheroidal satellites

**Relevance:** 10
**Novelty:** 8

## 2. [The ISM-CGM Transition of a Star-forming Dwarf Galaxy Resolved in Ionized Gas Emission](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.17480) <a id="link2"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2611.17480

**Authors:** Chloe Neufeld, Pieter van Dokkum, Imad Pasha

**Abstract:** The circumgalactic media (CGM) of dwarf galaxies are expected to be tightly coupled to their star formation, as low virial temperatures imply a CGM rich in cold accretion and shallow potential wells allow for more effective feedback. In this work, we use Keck/KCWI and KCRM to spatially resolve the ionized gas at the boundary between the interstellar medium (ISM) and CGM in dwarf galaxy UGC 6578 (M* = 10^7.8 Msun), out to ~0.1 Rvir. We discover a clear transition between the ISM and CGM at 0.033 Rvir, where the Halpha surface brightness profile changes from an exponential to a shallow power law, marking the first such detection at M*/Msun < 10^8. BPT diagnostics and ionization budget calculations indicate that the gas in the inner CGM is likely ionized by the galaxy's own star formation activity rather than shocks or the UV background. Spatially resolved emission maps also reveal a kinematically distinct, low-metallicity clump embedded in the inner CGM, and we discuss the possibility that this is a direct detection of ongoing gas accretion. This work serves as a pilot study for the Baryon Recycling in Dwarf Galaxies Emission Survey (BRIDGES), which will combine spatially resolved KCWI observations of the ISM-CGM interface in low-mass galaxies with wide-field observations of diffuse ionized gas out to the virial radius with the MOTHRA array.

**Comment:** Spatially resolved ISM-CGM transition in a star-forming dwarf

**Relevance:** 10
**Novelty:** 8

## 3. [Effects of Environment on the Star Formation Histories and Shapes of Simulated Galaxies](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.17427) <a id="link3"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2611.17427

**Authors:** Francisco J. Mercado, Brian A. Giraud Calderon, Jorge Moreno, Coral Wheeler, Jose A. Benavides, Pratik J. Gandhi, Tyler Smith, Olti Myrtaj, Robert Feldmann

**Abstract:** We investigate how environment is connected to the formation times and three-dimensional stellar shapes of galaxies using the FIREbox cosmological volume simulation. We analyze 1720 resolved galaxies at $z=0$ spanning stellar masses $M_\star\approx5\times10^6$ to $5\times10^{11}\,M_\odot$ and environments ranging from isolated galaxies to groups in $\sim10^{13}\,M_\odot$ halos. We quantify environment using the Perturbation Index (PI), which measures the cumulative tidal influence of neighboring galaxies relative to each galaxy's own binding force. We measure formation times from cumulative star formation histories and shapes from intrinsic stellar axis ratios. Formation times depend most strongly on stellar mass, but below $10^{10}\,M_\odot$, galaxies in more strongly perturbing environments formed their stars earlier, most clearly in the late-time star formation of low-mass galaxies. Galaxies in these environments also have larger minor-to-major axis ratios at all masses: more spherical at low and intermediate mass and thicker along the minor axis at high mass. A partial-correlation analysis shows that, below $10^8\,M_\odot$, the environment-shape correlation is no longer significant at fixed formation time, whereas between $10^8$ and $10^{10}\,M_\odot$ a modest correlation remains. Above $10^{10}\,M_\odot$, the weak environment-shape correlation is not explained by formation time. Below $10^{10}\,M_\odot$, high-PI galaxies also have lower halo masses at fixed stellar mass; at fixed halo mass, environment is only weakly correlated with formation time and shape, whereas formation time and shape remain correlated. We therefore predict that, at fixed stellar mass, dwarf galaxies in stronger tidal environments should appear rounder in projection, with the difference largely tracking the ages of their stellar populations.

**Comment:** Environmental effects on dwarf galaxy SFHs in FIREbox

**Relevance:** 8
**Novelty:** 7

## 4. [2D and 3D unified RHD atmospheres and wind models of early B-supergiants including approximate NLTE effects](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.18162) <a id="link4"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2611.18162

**Authors:** Dwaipayan Debnath, Jon O. Sundqvist, Nicolas Moens

**Abstract:** B supergiants are evolved massive stars with low surface gravities and lower effective temperatures, with substantial radiation-driven winds. Generally, they have been studied assuming 1D spherical symmetry. We present the first multi-D unified atmosphere and wind models for B supergiants, especially investigating the effects of approximate NLTE ionization and subsurface turbulence on the atmosphere and wind dynamics. We computed multi-D box-in-a-star RHD unified atmosphere and wind simulations of 2D/3D early B supergiant models using a hybrid opacity approach. We calculated line opacities from approximate NLTE populations, including line scattering. We calculated the flux-, energy-, and Planck-mean line opacities separately from our large line database using Sobolev escape probabilities, assuming two-level atoms. The models develop a highly turbulent atmosphere with time-averaged turbulent velocities of ~ 40 - 55 km/s at the photosphere in the 2D simulations, and ~ 25 km/s in 3D. In the cooler B1, B1.5 models, the wind acceleration is more gradual than in the hotter B0 model, leading to a significantly higher maximum wind speed in the B0 model. Further, the approximate NLTE formalism strongly alters the ionization stratification in the wind compared to LTE, modifying the line force. Mass-loss rates obtained in the models are of order 10^-6 - 3 x 10^-6 solar masses per year. Including turbulent pressure in hydrodynamically consistent NLTE CMF steady-state 1D simulations with comparable parameters can produce higher mass-loss rates and lower terminal wind velocities than in corresponding models without turbulent velocity, in overall qualitative agreement with our multi-D simulations. Our calculations suggest that in the lower surface-gravity B supergiants, in addition to modifying the photospheric density scale heights, turbulent pressure also influences global properties of the wind.

**Comment:** New multi-D stellar atmosphere and wind models for supergiants

**Relevance:** 8
**Novelty:** 7

## 5. [Hydrodynamic and photoionization analysis of a Cosmic Dawn dwarf galaxy based on a JWST-inferred star formation history](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.17582) <a id="link5"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2611.17582

**Authors:** B. Melekh, S. Chudyk, I. Koshmak

**Abstract:** We investigate the evolution of the emergent spectrum of the Cosmic Dawn dwarf galaxy 2779 using coupled hydrodynamics and photoionization modeling. Evolutionary sequences of spherically symmetric, multi-component models track the star-formation history -- derived from JWST data -- from $z \approx 29.5$ to $9.5$. BPASS stellar population synthesis is coupled with the evolution of the superwind bubble and the surrounding nebular environment. The emergent spectra include escaping stellar radiation, nebular lines and continuum, as well as X-ray emission from the hot superwind cavity. At early stages, the nebular shell suppresses the escape of Lyman-continuum (Lyc) photons, while allowing X-ray emission to emerge. Significant Lyc escape appears near $z \approx 20$, followed by a non-monotonic evolution of the escape fraction ($f_{\text{esc}}$) and the X-ray luminosity per unit star-formation rate ($L_X/\text{SFR}$). Including diffuse ionizing radiation from the nebula can result in an effective escape fraction $f_{\text{esc}} > 1$. The model sequences terminate at a stage of stagnation or the complete displacement of the outer nebular component into a shell. A comparison with JWST photometry indicates that the final model spectrum generally reproduces the shape of the observed spectral energy distribution, although the overall flux level is too high. An illustrative comparison with a previous model having a lower stellar mass indicates that accounting for the evolving contribution of the nebula can reduce the stellar mass required to match observations. However, a more precise determination of stellar mass requires a self-consistent refinement of the star formation history. These models serve as a basis for constructing representative grids of models, as well as for conducting careful checks on whether dwarf galaxies could have driven the reionization of the Universe on their own.

**Comment:** Superwind bubble outflows and photoionization in a dwarf galaxy

**Relevance:** 7
**Novelty:** 6

## 6. [Improved Bounds on the Dark Matter-Neutrino Cross Section using LSBGs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.17922) <a id="link6"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2611.17922

**Authors:** Akari Suezaki, Shunsaku Horiuchi

**Abstract:** We derive new constraints on the interaction cross section between dark matter and neutrinos ($\sigma_{\nu\chi}$) that improve upon existing limits. We adopt a mechanism where neutrinos emitted from core-collapse supernovae transfer their energy to the dark matter halo via elastic scattering, transitioning a centrally cusped profile to a flat-cored profile. We apply this mechanism to Low Surface Brightness Galaxies (LSBGs), which are characterized by their relatively large sizes, low stellar densities, and measurements of dark matter cores. We improve upon previous methods so that each dark matter particle is counted at most once, which weakens existing limits, e.g., from the Fornax dSph by a factor of $\sim 50$. Applying our new method to LSBGs, we derive an upper limit of $\sigma_{\nu\chi}< 8.5 \times 10^{-25}\,\rm cm^2$ for a dark matter mass of $m_\chi =1\,\rm GeV$ at a typical supernova neutrino energy of $E_\nu=15\,\rm MeV$. This constraint is approximately three orders of magnitude stronger than the Fornax limit derived with the same method and slightly stronger than the bound from the dark matter diffused supernova neutrino background (DMDSNB).

**Comment:** Dark matter core-cusp transition in dwarf galaxies and LSBGs

**Relevance:** 7

## 7. [Properties of near-surface turbulence in hot, massive stars: Statistical signatures from multi-dimensional models of O-type stars](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.18254) <a id="link7"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2611.18254

**Authors:** Cassandra Van der Sijpt, Florian Driessen, Jon Sundqvist, Nicolas Moens, Dwaipayan Debnath

**Abstract:** Variability of massive stars indicates the presence of complex, multi-D physics near the surface. Recent advancements in multi-D radiation-hydrodynamical modelling of massive star atmospheres show strong turbulence triggered by the iron opacity bump. We analyze statistical properties of sub-surface turbulence in multi-D simulations of O-star atmospheres. Particularly, we study potential dependencies of turbulent properties on global stellar parameters. Using 2D O-star models, we studied several statistical properties of the turbulence that develops in these models. We compute autocorrelation functions of density and velocity to study typical eddy length scales. Moreover, we compute kinetic energy power spectra to analyze the energy distribution and compare this to known results from established turbulence theories. We find anisotropy of the turbulent eddies in velocity space. Moreover, this anisotropy increases with classical Eddington factor. This anisotropy disappears in density space where all models show nearly isotropic structures and no dependence on stellar parameters. The kinetic energy power spectra exhibit an inertial range with a cascade exponent that has an average value of -2.57. This exponent does not show any clear dependence on stellar parameters. Importantly, this energy cascade is significantly steeper than those in known turbulence theories such as Kolmogorov turbulence with exponent -5/3 and Burgers turbulence with exponent -2. This additional steepening can potentially be attributed to radiative damping which increases with increasing wavenumber. Sub-surface turbulence in hot, massive stars is anisotropic and radially dominated in velocity space and its energy cascade is steeper than both Kolmogorov turbulence and Burgers turbulence. Although not rigorously proven here, we suggest that this steepening is due to the scale-dependent effect of radiative damping.

**Relevance:** 6
**Novelty:** 6

## 8. [Observations of stellar feedback: tracers, diagnostics, measurements, and physical interpretation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.17592) <a id="link8"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2611.17592

**Authors:** Anna F. McLeod

**Abstract:** Stellar feedback is central to galaxy evolution, yet the coupling of radiation, stellar winds, and supernovae to the multi-phase interstellar medium remains uncertain. This review treats this as a problem of inference, framed around four qualitative axes of ignorance: observational diagnostics are indirect; stellar population synthesis models must be ground-truthed before being used to infer feedback quantities; recent high-redshift observations have opened a new regime that may require re-calibration; and stellar feedback and the affected environment are interdependent. We examine the inference chain, from observables to physical quantities and from those quantities to how energy and momentum are transferred to the surrounding medium, across the principal diagnostics of stellar feedback: ionised gas, mechanically-driven bubbles, giant molecular cloud lifetimes, galactic outflows, and Lyman-continuum escape. We ask how much observations reveal about the link between the feedback-driving massive stars and the feedback-driven interstellar medium. We further discuss how stellar population synthesis assumptions, environment (e.g., metallicity, dust content), and galaxy properties (e.g., stellar mass, gas content) affect feedback measurements, and how high-redshift observations are testing calibrations established in the nearby Universe. The emerging picture is that interpreting a feedback measurement requires explicit control of the inference chain and of its physical context; feedback should therefore be contextualised, i.e. interpreted in terms of mechanism, gas phase, environment, and spatial and temporal scale. Progress will depend on measuring feedback-related quantities across the full range of star-forming systems and, more importantly, on obtaining observations (real, and synthetic from post-processed simulations) that match the same tracer and spatial scale across the same systems.

**Relevance:** 6
**Novelty:** 5

## 9. [Asteroseismology of blue straggler stars in $\omega$ Centauri. Stellar collisions as a possible formation scenario](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.17995) <a id="link9"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2611.17995

**Authors:** Jadwiga Daszynska-Daszkiewicz, P. Gora, P. Walczak, A. Miszuda, W. Szewczuk, M. Heller, F. R. N. Schneider

**Abstract:** Context. Blue stragglers are intriguing stars that appear more massive and younger than expected from single-star evolution, yet their evolutionary histories remain elusive because of the multiple possible formation channels. A promising and poorly explored avenue for addressing this problem is asteroseismology, which provides the most stringent constraints on internal structure and evolution.   Aims. We investigate five SX Phoenicis stars from the core of the globular cluster \omega Centauri that pulsate in two radial modes and occupy the blue-straggler region of the colour-magnitude diagram. Seismic models are constructed assuming a collisional origin and compared with our previous seismic studies based on single-star evolution. For comparison, we also explore a binary-evolution scenario involving mass transfer for one of the stars.   Methods. The evolution of the parent stars is computed with MESA. The collision is then modelled with the MMAMS code, which reconstructs the merger remnant using an entropic variable. The subsequent evolution is followed with MESA up to the current age of the cluster. Finally, we construct seismic models reproducing the observed frequencies of the two radial modes.   Results. We present the first seismic modelling of SX Phe stars (blue stragglers) based on the collision-formation scenario. Convective-core overshooting is essential for rejuvenation and for obtaining seismic models in the main-sequence phase of evolution, but it is not always sufficient. Whether a merger remnant undergoes rejuvenation depends in a complex way on the masses of the parent stars, metallicity, and collision time. We identify three evolutionary behaviours: no, delayed, and immediate rejuvenation. The collisional seismic models imply younger ages and higher masses than the seismic models based on single-star evolution.

**Relevance:** 5
**Novelty:** 6

## 10. [Unsupervised learning reveals a small number of galaxy formation pathways in IllustrisTNG](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.17559) <a id="link10"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2611.17559

**Authors:** C. Alvarez-Fernandez, H. Dominguez Sanchez, J. Vega-Ferrero, E. Angeloudi, A. Ferre-Mateu, D. Sotillo-Ramos

**Abstract:** Understanding the main drivers of galaxy formation and the connection between observable properties and evolutionary pathways is a central goal of extragalactic astronomy. Observations provide single-epoch snapshots of galaxies at different stages of their development, whereas cosmological simulations allow us to trace the full formation histories of individual systems within a theoretical framework. Extracting information from these datasets remains challenging. Our aim is to use machine-learning techniques to unveil physical properties from the simulation datasets. We apply two unsupervised ML techniques, dimensionality reduction and clustering, to identify galaxy evolution key parameters. We use UMAP as our dimensionality reduction tool and k-means as our clustering method. We perform dimensionality reduction on the star formation histories (SFHs) of a sample of aproximately 10000 galaxies drawn from the TNG50 and TNG100 simulations. The resulting representation of the SFHs in a two-dimensional UMAP space reveals a clear ordering of galaxy mass assembly histories, separating quenched and star-forming systems, as well as galaxies with significant ex situ stellar mass contributions. Galaxy properties from simulation catalogs that are not used in the dimensionality reduction show correlations across the UMAP plane. Furthermore, using a k-means clustering analysis, we identify six groups of galaxies with distinct evolutionary histories. These groups show systematic differences in stellar mass, morphology, angular momentum, star formation activity, and merger history, and occupy different regions of the SFR-mass and mass-size planes. Our results indicate that the diversity of galaxy formation histories in IllustrisTNG can be organized into a small number of characteristic evolutionary pathways linking galaxy accretion histories and present-day structural and star-forming properties.

**Relevance:** 5
**Novelty:** 6

## 11. [Augmenting Galaxy Catalogues using Machine Learning in the FLAMINGO Simulations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.17425) <a id="link11"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2611.17425

**Authors:** Elliot Scott, Marika Asgari, Joop Schaye, Matthieu Schaller, Markus Michael Rau

**Abstract:** Hydrodynamic simulations are instrumental in the analysis of observations such as galaxy clustering used for the estimation of cosmological parameters. However, for these purposes, the simulations need both a large box size and high resolution, which is computationally prohibitive. In this work we use the connection between galaxies and their host dark matter halos to populate dark-matter-only simulations, using data from the FLAMINGO simulations suite. We employ the Extremely Randomized Trees algorithm to model the connection between the mass profile, history and environment of dark matter halos and the probability distribution of the stellar mass of the galaxy contained in the halo. We use halos matched between a dark-matter-only simulation and the corresponding hydrodynamical simulation to train a model for centrals. For satellites, we employ a Gaussian Processes emulator that simultaneously optimises the hyperparameters of a model and selects for the dark matter features that are predictive of the stellar mass while being insensitive to the differences between the hydrodynamic and dark-matter-only simulations, then train a model on only these features. We then apply these models to produce a new galaxy catalogue for the 2.8 Gpc FLAMINGO-10k dark-matter-only simulation as a product of this work. We demonstrate that this catalogue reproduces the statistics of the galaxy-halo connection predicted by the smaller volume hydrodynamical simulation on which the model was trained, including the stellar mass function, stellar mass-dark matter mass relation, 2-point correlation functions and correlations between stellar mass and dark matter concentration, half mass radius and dark matter mass within 3 Mpc.

**ArXiv ID:** 2611.17628

**Authors:** Chow-Choong Ngeow, Anupam Bhardwaj

**Abstract:** The Vera C. Rubin Observatory's Legacy Survey of Space Time (LSST) is expected to discover numerous Mira variables in nearby galaxies, enabling their application for the cosmic distance scale. Miras are excellent distance indicators at infrared wavelengths, and therefore their period-luminosity (PL) relations in reddest $zy$ photometric systems will be crucial in the LSST era. We present first empirical $zy$-band PL relation for 85 oxygen-rich (O-rich) Miras located in the Large Magellanic Cloud based on time-series observations collected using the Dark Energy Camera (DECam) at the V\'ictor M. Blanco 4-m Telescope. The DECam photometry was directly calibrated using the Rubin Observatory Data Preview 2 (DP2) data. The observed PL relations were found to exhibit a break around a period of $\sim 300$ days, and therefore we only derived and calibrated the PL relations for Miras with a period shorter than $300$ days. We also explored the Rubin LSST DP2 data for known Mira variables in three Local Group dwarf galaxies. The $zy$-band light curves of Miras in IC 1613 are too sparsely sampled, whereas the DP2 observations of NGC 6822 and the Sagittarius Dwarf Irregular Galaxy cover only a short temporal baseline. For an O-rich Mira in NGC 6822, we obtain a distance modulus $\mu = 23.32\pm0.24$~mag in good agreement with the literature. Significantly improved temporal coverage is expected in the first official Rubin LSST data release that will enable a robust validation of the PL relation in these nearby systems.

## 13. [Predicting Individual Dark Matter Halo Concentrations from Their Assembly Histories](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.17676) <a id="link13"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2611.17676

**Authors:** Dao-Zhou Wang, Weipeng Lin, Weishan Zhu, Tian-Cheng Luan

**Abstract:** We present an empirical model for predicting concentration parameters of individual dark matter halos in cold and warm dark matter (WDM) cosmologies over z=0-5. Constructed using a large suite of cosmological N-body simulations, our model separately incorporates cosmic expansion (linear growth factor), mass scale (linear density fluctuations), halo mass assembly histories via the Extended Press-Schechter (EPS) scaled formation variable, plus a WDM damping term tied to the half-mode mass. It works for both virial and 200 $\rho_{crit}$ halo definitions. Across all tested cosmologies and redshifts, our global logarithmic fit gives concentration scatter ~0.09 dex for relaxed halos and ~0.09 to 0.10 dex for all halos. While relaxed halos maintain nearly constant scatter near 0.09 dex, the scatter of all halos at z>3 increases from 0.10 to 0.18 dex, revealing substantial violent-relaxation impacts on high-redshift halo structure. At fixed mass, concentration scatter is dominated by assembly history. We extract the scaled formation variable's probability distribution function (PDF) from merger trees and find departures from standard EPS theory. This PDF is narrower in WDM than LCDM due to suppressed small-scale perturbations and rapid, delayed monolithic collapse near the WDM truncation scale. Monte Carlo realizations with our formulae reproduce concentration distributions and match our prior peak-concentration model, whereas standard EPS underestimates concentrations from delayed-formation bias. Our framework offers a transparent, computationally lightweight tool to predict individual halo concentrations across diverse cosmological models.

**Novelty:** 6

## 14. [Model Choice or Model Bias? How stellar spectral libraries and activity modelling choices influence Ariel spectra- I. Forward model](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.18107) <a id="link14"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2611.18107

**Authors:** N. -E. Nèmec, A. Thompson, Ò. Porqueras-León, S. Stucki, N. R. C. Gomes, D. Baroch, I. Ribas

**Abstract:** The Ariel mission will conduct a population-level survey of approximately 1000 exoplanet atmospheres across its nominal lifetime, requiring unprecedented consistency in the treatment of stellar contamination of transmission spectra. We present a systematic comparison of stellar spectral libraries and modelling approaches for computing facular and spot contamination spectra across the full Ariel wavelength range (0.5-7.8 microns) for six stellar spectral types spanning F3 to M2. Using a surface resolved forward model with approximately 200 randomly generated active region configurations per spectral type, we compute contamination spectra at Ariel Tier 3 resolution and compare three 1D spectral libraries against 3D MHD stellar atmosphere spectra as a physical ground truth. We find that library-dependent differences in the facular treatment introduce systematic biases exceeding the Ariel Tier 3 sensitivity threshold of approximately 20 ppm across most of the wavelength range, for most spectral types and activity levels considered. These biases align with the absorption bands of water, carbon dioxide, carbon monoxide, and methane, rendering them indistinguishable from genuine atmospheric signals at Ariel's precision. Our results demonstrate hat the choice of stellar spectral library, in particular the faculae treatment is a critical and currently underappreciated source of systematic error for Ariel atmospheric characterisation. The impact on atmospheric retrievals is quantified in a companion paper.

**Novelty:** 5

## 15. [Precise Deep-Learning Reconstruction of Galaxy Cluster Mass Maps and Substructures from Optical Observations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.18487) <a id="link15"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2611.18487

**Authors:** Atulit Srivastava, Weiguang Cui, Daniel de Andres, Guillaume Bonnet, Romeel Davé, Stefano Ettori, Massimo Meneghetti, Carlo Giocoli, Gustavo Yepes

**Abstract:** Accurately mapping the total mass distribution within galaxy clusters, including dark matter, is fundamental to understanding structure formation and constraining cosmological parameters. Conventional mass-mapping techniques rely predominantly on gravitational lensing and often produce smooth mass distributions based on analytical profiles, potentially missing important internal substructures. We introduce a complementary, simulation-based inference approach to reconstruct detailed galaxy cluster mass maps and resolve their internal substructures directly from optical imaging. We trained deep learning models on realistic hydrodynamical simulations of galaxy clusters to infer their mass distributions from optical observations. We subsequently applied this framework to observations of real galaxy clusters obtained with the Hubble Space Telescope (HST) as part of the Cluster Lensing And Supernova survey with Hubble (CLASH) programme. The reconstructed mass maps successfully reproduce the overall shapes and radial mass profiles of the CLASH clusters, showing strong agreement with independent gravitational lensing measurements. Furthermore, the subhalo mass functions derived from the reconstructed maps exhibit power-law behaviour broadly consistent with predictions from numerical simulations. Our results demonstrate that combining optical imaging with deep learning provides a scalable and physically meaningful approach to mapping galaxy cluster mass distributions. This framework opens a new avenue for exploiting upcoming wide-field imaging surveys to systematically investigate dark matter substructures across large cluster samples, complementing traditional gravitational lensing techniques. Moreover, the model is inherently flexible and can incorporate multi-wavelength observations, including X-ray and Sunyaev--Zel'dovich data, to further improve total mass predictions.

**Abstract:** Color--magnitude diagrams (CMDs) can constrain the progenitor masses of supernova remnants (SNRs), but mass estimates for massive stars are affected by limited temperature sensitivity, extinction, crowding, and nebular contamination. We use the Large Magellanic Cloud (LMC) SNR N63A and its OB association LH83 as a benchmark to evaluate CMD-based mass estimates. Among the tested diagnostics, the extinction-corrected MCPS $B$ vs. $U-B$ CMD provides the most useful constraint on the massive-star population. The $V$ vs. $B-V$ CMD is less reliable, while the reddening-free $W$-$Q$ diagram does not improve individual mass estimates because of its larger photometric uncertainties. Gaia EDR3 recovers bright stars missing or saturated in MCPS but provides weaker separation among the hottest stars. For seven spectroscopically classified stars, the $B$ vs. $U-B$ CMD-based masses are lower than the HR-diagram masses inferred using the same MIST tracks by $8.0~M_\odot$ on average, with a scatter of $7.0~M_\odot$. Isochrone comparisons suggest that most of the massive stars in LH83 formed about 7-15 Myr ago, complicating progenitor mass estimates based on the present-day mass function. Nevertheless, the masses of D243-1a and D243-2, stellar lifetimes predicted by the MIST models, and the empirical bubble-size-progenitor-mass relation suggest a progenitor mass of order 25-30 $M_\odot$. Our results highlight the potential and limitations of this approach for studying other LMC SNRs in OB associations.

## 17. [Probing baryonic feedback using fast radio bursts and galaxy surveys](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.18512) <a id="link17"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2611.18512

**Authors:** Haruki Ebina, Martin White

**Abstract:** The dispersion measure (DM) of fast radio bursts (FRB) provides a noisy measure of the free-electron column density to the burst. With next-generation FRB surveys the number of bursts will drastically increase and measurements of the baryon distribution will be possible on the Mpc scales most relevant to Stage IV weak lensing surveys. Comparing the DM auto-spectrum, DM-shear cross-spectrum and the kinetic Sunyaev-Zel'dovich effect, we argue that the cross-correlation of FRB catalogs with galaxy surveys offers many advantages. Hybrid Effective Field Theory (HEFT), often used in large-scale structure cosmology, is capable of modeling the clustering of electrons jointly with galaxies and matter to small scales. Using HEFT with the Pad\'e model of baryonic feedback, we forecast that next-generation FRB surveys (combined with galaxy samples) will be able to constrain feedback at a level informative for Stage IV weak lensing surveys. In particular we should not be limited by modeling the manner in which galaxies trace the matter field. Rather constraints of the higher-order electron biases and the electron-galaxy cross-stochasticity provide the dominant limitation, and are an obvious target for improvement in order to reach percent-level constraints. The same measurements also provide a valuable means of constraining feedback in hydrodynamic simulations of galaxy formation in a way that is not tied to the modeling of particular galaxy samples.

**Relevance:** 3
**Novelty:** 6

---

## 18. [Detectability limits of Ce abundances in Gaia RVS](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.17591) <a id="link18"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2611.17591

**Authors:** S. Vitali, A. Escorza, T. Signor, P. A. Palicio, M. Abdul-Masih, C. Allende Prieto, D. Godoy-Rivera, J. I. González Hernández, M. Schultheis, S. Shetye, S. Van Eck

**Abstract:** Gaia DR3 provides medium-resolution Radial Velocity Spectrograph (RVS) spectra for millions of stars, but the extent to which they contain recoverable cerium (Ce) information across stellar parameter space remains unclear. We assess Ce detectability in Gaia RVS spectra and the reliability of DR3 Ce abundances ahead of the larger DR4 catalogue. We compute synthetic spectra across a stellar-parameter grid representative of the Gaia RVS sample. We combine the Cayrel equivalent-width criterion and the Cram\'er-Rao bound to map Ce detectability as a function of stellar parameters and signal-to-noise (SNR). We also quantify continuum-placement errors and compare the predicted detectability boundary with the Gaia DR3 GSP-Spec catalogue. Ce is reliably detectable primarily in cool giants at [Fe/H] $\gtrsim$ 0.5 dex and with at least [Ce/Fe] $\gtrsim$ 0 at an average SNR of 50, and the detectability region expands toward higher gravities at increasing SNR. Continuum-placement errors of $\gtrsim$1% become a significant source of bias, and at $\gtrsim$2% they completely prevent Ce detection. The criterion we present identifies where RVS Ce abundances, including those from DR4, can be robustly exploited. Outside our detectability region, these abundances should be interpreted cautiously, whether derived by GSP-Spec or by supervised methods. Currently, depending on the assumed continuum-placement uncertainty, between $\sim$40% and $\sim$70% of the published DR3 Ce measurements fall within this region; however, the rest are not reliable. Our findings are particularly important for metal-poor stars, for which the Ce lines in the RVS range are simply not detectable.

**Relevance:** 3
**Novelty:** 5

---

## 19. [Properties of stellar disks and spheroids in simulated galaxies](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.17415) <a id="link19"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2611.17415

**Authors:** Valeria A. Cristiani, Julio F. Navarro, Mario G. Abadi

**Abstract:** The optical morphology of a galaxy is determined by the relative importance of their disk and spheroidal components. Although the scaling laws of disk-dominated or spheroid-dominated galaxies have been studied in some detail, less attention has been paid to the properties of each component in galaxies where both coexist. We use a dynamical decomposition of 3452 galaxies from the TNG100 simulations with stellar masses in the range $10 \leq \log_\mathrm{10}(M_*/M_\odot) \leq 12$ to study how, at given mass, the properties of a spheroid are altered by the presence of a disk, and vice versa. We define spheroids as the non-rotating component made up of all stars in retrograde motion, plus an equal number of co-rotating stars mirrored in angular momentum and energy. Disks simply consist of the remainder of (co-rotating) stars. We find that disk properties (rotation speed, size, formation time) depend sensitively on the mass of the spheroidal component and therefore of the total mass of the galaxy in which both reside. At given mass, disks rotate faster, are more extended, and form their stars earlier as the mass of the spheroidal component increases. On the other hand, at fixed mass, the properties of spheroids are less sensitive to the presence of a disk. Roughly speaking, the classical bulges of simulated disk galaxies closely resemble ellipticals of similar stellar mass. The apparent structural invariance of spheroids to the presence of a disk is somewhat surprising, but it likely results because disks form out of high angular momentum material which surrounds the spheroid and thus have less dynamical influence on a spheroid's structure.

**Relevance:** 3
**Novelty:** 5

A. Walton (Institute of Astronomy, University of Cambridge, Madingley Road, Cambridge CB3 0HA, UK), A. H. Wright (Ruhr University Bochum, Faculty of Physics and Astronomy, Astronomical Institute)

**Abstract:** Euclid is expected to discover ~$10^5$ galaxy-galaxy strong gravitational lenses across ~14,000deg$^2$, requiring automated searches with high completeness and low false-positive rates. Despite advances in deep learning, the effects of training-set composition, image representation, and preprocessing on survey-scale detection remain poorly understood. We present an ablation study of the convolutional neural network used in the ECLIPSE strong-lens detection pipeline, evaluating different training configurations on a fixed test set. Using simulated and real Euclid data, we compare VIS-only and pseudo-RGB representations, FITS and JPEG formats, training-set composition, hard-negative selection, and multi-stage training. We also assess ensembling on ~67 million DR1 cutouts. Training-set composition and strategy strongly affect high-Recall performance. Omitting simulation based pre-training reduces high-Recall weighted average Precision by 83.7% for VIS-only and 74.5% for pseudo-RGB models. Negative-sample selection also substantially affects performance, while FITS yields modest gains over JPEG at high Recall (~2-5%). On DR1, the logit-mean ensemble improves average Precision by 28.0% and 39.9% over the individual VIS-only and pseudo-RGB models. Among ~70,000 candidates selected for visual inspection, it recovers 11,256 unique grade A (secure) and B (probable) lenses, compared with 9650 and 10,395 for the individual models. The branches show complementary ranking preferences, particularly for lenses benefiting from near-infrared information. Careful training-set design, realistic negatives, and multi-band ensembling improve survey-scale searches. Our final ensemble achieves ~80% Recall at the operational selection threshold, outperforming ablation models with higher global classification metrics and highlighting the need to optimise performance for realistic survey conditions.[Abridged]

Richards, A. Ali, B. Jamieson, C. Bronner, D. Horiguchi, A. Minamino, Y. Sasaki

**Abstract:** The detection of solar neutrino provides direct evidence for on-going fusion in the core of the Sun and serve as a probe for that solar core. Production rate of $^8$B is sensitive to the solar core temperature. Adiabatic flavor conversion of $^8$B solar neutrinos depends on the local solar electron density. Combined with the roughly exponential decrease in solar electron density with increasing distance from the center of the solar sphere, the solar core size, density, and temperature are inferred. This article presents an outer bound on solar core size and measurements of the density and temperature in the solar core based on Super-Kamiokande phase I-IV solar neutrino data. An outer bound is set for the solar core at $90\%$ confidence level as $0.499R_\odot$ for the high metallicity B16+GS98 Standard Solar Model and $0.381R_\odot$ for K2-A09-MZvar-12 which includes protosolar accretion. A combined fit with the SNO polynomial coefficients improves the limit to $0.268R_\odot$ for B16+GS98 and $0.215R_\odot$ for K2-A09-MZvar-12. Results for other metallicity cases are also reported. In the high metallicity scenario assuming nominal solar core density and solar core size, temperature at the solar center is reported at $T_\text{C}=(15.599\pm0.0016)\times10^6$ K and $T_{\text{npz}}=(15.492\pm0.0017)\times10^6$ K at the peak radius of the $^8$B production profile.

## 22. [What constrains the neutron-star crust? Present data, future measurements, and EOS doppelgängers](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.17920) <a id="link22"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2611.17920

**Authors:** Vishal Parmar

**Abstract:** The neutron-star crust plays a central role in pulsar glitches, thermal evolution, and stellar oscillations, yet its properties remain difficult to constrain. We use Bayesian inference with a unified crust-core equation of state to assess what present nuclear and astrophysical data tell us about the crust and what future stellar measurements can add. Within this model, present crust constraints are dominated by chiral effective field theory, with laboratory data providing additional information and current stellar observations having a smaller effect. The adopted theoretical bands determine how tightly the crust properties are constrained, while the treatment of neutron-rich surface energy affects their inferred values. Forecasts centred on a single reference equation of state show that more precise measurements of radius, tidal deformability, and total moment of inertia mainly constrain crust thickness. They provide little additional information about the crust-core transition, crust mass, or crustal moment of inertia and do not recover the precision lost when the chiral effective field theory constraint is removed. Two models with nearly identical global stellar observables can have crust masses and crustal moments of inertia differing by about a factor of two, yet remain indistinguishable at the assumed precision. Precise global measurements therefore need not uniquely determine the crust. Observations that directly probe the crust are needed to distinguish these models.

## 23. [Restoring Consistency with Poynting's Theorem in Numerical MHD and RMHD](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.18508) <a id="link23"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2611.18508

**Authors:** Dinshaw S. Balsara, Deepak Bhoriya

**Abstract:** For perfectly conducting plasmas, Poynting's theorem states that the time rate of change of electromagnetic energy density equals the negative of the divergence of the Poynting flux. To the best of our knowledge, this involutive theorem has never been mimetically incorporated in codes. We show that there is an inconsistency in MHD and RMHD codes that evolve face-centered magnetic fields in globally-divergence-free fashion. This is because the face-centered flux of energy density, which incorporates a Poynting vector, is evaluated using one-dimensional Riemann solvers that are applied at face-centers. However, the face-centered magnetic fields are evolved according to edge-centered electric fields that come from two-dimensional Riemann solvers that are applied to the edges of a zone. While edge-centered electric fields are used to evolve the magnetic field, they have no influence on the face-centered Poynting flux. For situations where the magnetic energy is much larger than the thermal energy, this causes a negative pressure to be evaluated at the zone-centers. This paper restores consistency by using edge-centered variables to evaluate a face-centered Poynting flux. A simple accuracy-preserving correction is made to the face-centered flux of energy density. We show that it helps considerably in simulations that have very strong magnetic fields.

**Novelty:** 6

## 24. [Mapping the Evolving Cosmic Web on the Lightcone with WebON: HOLI Mock Strategy](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.17385) <a id="link24"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2611.17385

**Authors:** Francisco-Shu Kitaura, Francesco Sinigaglia, Kilian Peraza Vera, Pere Rosselló, Daniel Forero-Sánchez, Natalia Villa-Nova Rodrigues, Jorge E. García-Farieta, Yunyi Tang, Jose María Coloma-Nadal, Ginevra Favole, Aurelio Carnero-Rosell, Cheng Zhao, Kentaro Nagamine

**Abstract:** Wide-area spectroscopic surveys such as DESI require large ensembles of mock catalogues that follow gravitational evolution and tracer populations across the observer's past lightcone. We present WebON (Web ON the lightcone), a fast method for mapping the evolving dark-matter cosmic web onto the lightcone without periodic-box replication or the stitching of separately evolved snapshot shells. Displacements computed at a limited set of redshifts are combined with a particle-wise Zel'dovich estimate of the emission redshift and a continuous-redshift correction, allowing lightcone positions to be generated in a single forward pass. The method supports regularised ALPT and eALPT gravity solvers, with an optional short-range ridging correction. We illustrate full-sky matter and cosmic-web evolution and find percent-level agreement between lightcone and fixed-redshift angular power spectra over the tested redshift and multipole ranges, providing an internal consistency check of the lightcone construction. Optional modules address approximate relativistic position corrections and local primordial non-Gaussianity. Finally, we show how the spectral cosmic-web hierarchy connects WebON to the HOLI (Higher Order LIghtcone) mock strategy, in which post-processing ridging and calibrated HICOBIAN bias and subgrid prescriptions can recover tracer clustering beyond the nominal resolution of the ALPT matter field. Independent comparisons of ridged LPT matter fields with Abacus N -body simulations and published percent-level tests of the hierarchical tracer-bias model complement the lightcone consistency measurements presented here.

## 25. [Depletion of Dark Matter Spikes by Massive Black Hole Mergers](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.13170) <a id="link25"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2611.13170

**Authors:** Charlie Sharpe, Thomas F. M. Spieksma, Yonadav Barry Ginat, Ricarda S. Beckmann, Alexander Heger, Bence Kocsis

**Abstract:** Dark matter over-densities around massive black holes, known as dark matter spikes, can dephase gravitational waves emitted by intermediate- and extreme-mass-ratio inspirals (IMRIs/EMRIs), offering a promising way to indirectly detect dark matter. This detection channel, however, requires the spike to survive over cosmological time-scales. We investigate the impact that a host black hole's merger history has on its dark-matter spike. We focus on how the inspiral of a massive-black-hole (MBH) binary modifies and disrupts these spikes within the central $\sim 100$ Schwarzschild radii of the black hole. We find that a single merger with mass ratio $q = 0.03$ is capable of reducing a spike's density by around 3 (5) orders of magnitude at 100 (5) Schwarzschild radii across all polar angles relative to the binary's plane, independent of the primary MBH's mass, with larger mass ratios inducing even stronger depletion. In fact, a single past merger with mass ratio as small as $q = 0.005$ reduces central densities by 2--3 orders of magnitude, making gravitational-wave dephasing difficult to detect. We relate these results to the MBH merger history by analysing cosmological simulation data from Horizon-AGN. We find that mergers become increasingly common as the MBH mass increases, with mergers of mass ratio q > 0.03 having occurred in 50% of MBHs of mass $3\times10^7M_\odot$ and 95% of mass $10^8M_\odot$ at z=0. These fractions are reached at even lighter masses ($2\times10^7M_\odot$ and $5\times10^7M_\odot$, respectively) at z=3. We find that accretion-induced adiabatic MBH growth is unable to cause appreciable spike re-growth between such mergers in {\tt Horizon-AGN}. Thus, in combination with previous studies of EMRI-induced depletion, this work constrains the mass range over which massive black holes can host detectable DM spikes to $10^6M_\odot$--$5 \times 10^7M_\odot$ for $z \leq 3$.

## 26. [bihalofit-JAX: A Differentiable Code for the Nonlinear Matter Bispectrum and its Projected Statistics](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.18281) <a id="link26"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2611.18281

**Authors:** Alexander Roskill, Matteo Zennaro

**Abstract:** Theoretical predictions for the nonlinear matter bispectrum and related projected statistics have been historically very expensive to evaluate, leading many analyses to rely on emulators to speed up slow numerical codes. We show in this paper that this need not be the case: improved numerical techniques can be leveraged to directly evaluate predictions for the nonlinear matter bispectrum and projected statistics at speeds preferable for inference. We present bihalofit-JAX, an open-source, fully differentiable code for the nonlinear matter bispectrum based on the BiHalofit fitting formula, which includes numerous related projected statistics such as angular bispectra, skew spectra and aperture statistics. It computes $10000$ triangles of the matter bispectrum in $\sim1$--$2\ \rm{ms}$ per cosmology, one to three orders of magnitude faster than other implementations. It agrees numerically with the original BiHalofit code to better than $0.02\%$ on average over the cosmologies the formula was calibrated on, where the formula itself is accurate to $\sim20\%$ against simulations. It is also efficiently vectorised such that one can compute e.g. $500$ cosmologies in $\sim 2\ \rm{ms}$. This speed-up grows even more in the case of the third-order aperture statistics, which we compute in $\sim 6\ \rm{ms}$ for one cosmology, between $3\times10^3$ and $2.4\times10^4$ times faster than other publicly available codes. The pipeline is fully differentiable, and we demonstrate its performance in a simple inference problem, comparing the No-U-Turn Sampler (NUTS) to gradient-free inference with the affine-invariant ensemble sampler (emcee).

## 27. [Cubic Galileon Gravity on Nonlinear Scales with \texttt{Hi-COLA} for LSST Year 1](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.18211) <a id="link27"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2611.18211

**Authors:** C. M. A. Zanoletti, A. Sen Gupta, N. Ramachandra, T. Baker, B. Fiorini, K. Heitmann, D. Jones, C. D. Leonard, B. Li, N. Šarčević, for the LSST Dark Energy Science Collaboration

**Abstract:** Vera C. Rubin Observatory's Legacy Survey of Space and Time (LSST) is in a prime position to test Modified Gravity (MG) with Weak Lensing (WL) and Large Scale Structure (LSS). However, many nonlinear MG emulators in the literature enforce a $\Lambda$CDM background by design, missing where data suggest the most interesting deviations lie. We present an emulator for the 3D nonlinear matter power spectrum in Cubic Galileon gravity, a Horndeski theory with a single additional MG parameter, $f_\phi$, that modifies the background, growth, and lensing. Trained on \texttt{Hi-COLA} simulations with Gaussian Process Regression (GPR), the emulator achieves $\lesssim 2\%$ accuracy for $k \leq 1.2\ h\,\text{Mpc}^{-1}$ at the fiducial cosmology relative to \texttt{Hi-COLA}, and $\lesssim 5\%$ relative to full N-body \texttt{EFT-RAMSES} simulations. Additionally, conservative scale cuts excluding angular bins affected by baryonic physics enable unbiased parameter estimation in LSST Year 1 weak lensing and galaxy clustering ($3\times2$pt) forecasts, even when validated against simulated data based on higher-resolution N-body simulations. This demonstrates that, within a simplified LSST Y1 setup and the adopted scale cuts, \texttt{Hi-COLA}-based emulation is sufficient to develop inference infrastructure with Stage IV precision for Horndeski gravity models previously unmodeled on nonlinear scales.

## 28. [Effect of galaxy-informed merger timescales on massive black hole binaries in LISA](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.17409) <a id="link28"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2611.17409

**Authors:** Jaelyn S. Roth (Department of Physics and Astronomy, Vanderbilt University, Nashville, TN), Kelly Holley-Bockelmann (SRON Space Research Organisation Netherlands, Leiden, Netherlands, Department of Life and Physical Sciences, Fisk University, Nashville, TN, Department of Physics and Astronomy, Vanderbilt University, Nashville, TN), Michael Tremmel (School of Physics, University College Cork, Cork, Ireland), Jillian Bellovary (Department of Physics, Queensborough Community College, Bayside, NY, Department of Astrophysics, American Museum of Natural History, New York, NY, Astrophysics Program, CUNY Graduate Center, New York, NY), Riddhi Patel (Department of Physics and Astronomy, Vanderbilt University, Nashville, TN, Department of Life and Physical Sciences, Fisk University, Nashville, TN), Andrea Derdzinski (Department of Physics and Astronomy, Vanderbilt University, Nashville, TN, Department of Life and Physical Sciences, Fisk University, Nashville, TN), KeShawn Ivory (Department of Physics and Astronomy, Vanderbilt University, Nashville, TN), Fazeel Mahmood Khan (New York University Abu Dhabi, Abu Dhabi, UAE, Center for Astrophysics and Space Science, NYUAD Research Institute, New York University Abu Dhabi, Abu Dhabi, UAE), Andrea Valerio Maccio (New York University Abu Dhabi, Abu Dhabi, UAE, Center for Astrophysics and Space Science, NYUAD Research Institute, New York University Abu Dhabi, Abu Dhabi, UAE)

**Abstract:** Over a decade of direct N-body simulations tell us that massive black hole binary (MBHB) merger timescales are strongly dependent on the structural and kinematic properties of the host galaxy merger remnant. The physical stages of stellar scattering and gravitational wave (GW) emission of MBH coalescence occur below the resolution limit of most cosmological simulations, requiring post-processing to achieve accurate merging times. We apply a recently developed galaxy-informed merger timescale for MBHBs to the Romulus25 simulation and compare with current MBH merger rate and event rate estimates for the upcoming Laser Interferometer Space Antenna (LISA). We find a median stellar scattering time of ~ 10^8.2 yr^-1, roughly an order of magnitude faster than those produced by timescales that rely on extrapolations of the stellar density profile. We predict a merger rate of ~ 11.5 yr^-1 corresponding to 46 merger events over a 4-year LISA mission. We also investigate the importance of the relative orbital inclination of the MBHB with respect to the host galaxy and of nuclear star cluster (NSC) modeling, finding that the quickest merger events correspond to MBHBs that counterrotate and galaxies assigned to host NSCs, which may be important for host galaxy identification in the multi-messenger era.

## 29. [Resonant Trapping of Neutrinophilic Dark Matter in Supernovae](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.17604) <a id="link29"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2611.17604

**Authors:** Christopher V. Cappiello, P. S. Bhupal Dev, Amol V. Patwardhan

**Abstract:** Supernova cooling provides some of the strongest constraints on light particles beyond the Standard Model, including sub-GeV dark matter (DM). While sufficiently large couplings to the Standard Model sector can trap these particles inside the supernova or cause them to decay before escaping, there typically exists an intermediate range of couplings in which their efficient production and subsequent escape lead to excessive energy loss, resulting in stringent constraints on new light particles. In this work, we demonstrate for the first time that, for certain types of DM-neutrino interactions, resonantly enhanced $s$-channel scattering with neutrinos can efficiently trap DM inside the supernova and can fundamentally alter the conventional supernova cooling constraints. In particular, we show that for mediator masses comparable to the supernova core temperature, this resonant scattering can prevent a substantial fraction of the produced DM from freely escaping, thereby significantly weakening, or even evading, the usual supernova cooling constraints on such models.

**Novelty:** 6

## 30. [Does Dark Energy Evolve $\textit{and}$ Cluster?](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.18557) <a id="link30"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2611.18557

**Authors:** Maria Luisa Gallavotti, Alessandra Silvestri

**Abstract:** Recent cosmological observations have renewed interest in dynamical dark energy, and naturally raise the question of whether departures from the standard expansion history are accompanied by modifications of gravitational dynamics. We address this question through a joint, non-parametric reconstruction of the effective dark-energy contribution to the expansion and the gravitational responses governing clustering and lensing, complemented by a combined covariance-mode analysis, without imposing model-specific relations between the sectors. Combining CMB, DESI DR2 BAO, and Pantheon+ supernova observations, and subsequently adding large scale structure observations including DES Year 3 $6\times2$pt data and other surveys, we recover a broad maximum in the effective dark-energy density near $z\simeq0.6$--$0.7$. The gravitational responses exhibit suggestive low-redshift enhancements over their GR values, approximately coincident with the background feature. Adding large scale structure information preserves the background feature but favours a mildly reduced lensing response. The combined covariance-mode analysis identifies the combinations of background and gravitational responses most tightly constrained by current observations and quantifies their departures from $\Lambda$CDM. The leading modes are decisively background dominated, while the gravitational sector remains less precisely constrained. Thus, present data cannot yet determine whether the suggestive low-redshift features in the two sectors share a common physical origin, a question that ongoing Stage-IV surveys will be able to address

**Relevance:** 2
**Novelty:** 6

---

## 31. [Multipoles in the IllustrisTNG simulation galaxies and implications for Strong Gravitational Lens modelling in Time-delay Cosmography](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.17538) <a id="link31"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2611.17538

**Authors:** Abdullah Al Zaif, Suhail Dhawan, Vasily Belokurov, Erin E. Hayes

**Abstract:** Time-delay cosmography is a powerful independent probe of the Hubble constant ($H_0$), yet reaching the sub-2$\%$ precision required to resolve the Hubble tension demands a rigorous treatment of mass model systematics. We use realistic massive elliptical galaxies from the IllustrisTNG hydrodynamic simulation suite to characterise higher-order angular mass complexity in the form of multipoles ($m=1,3,4$) and quantify their impact on $H_0$ inference. We find that these multipole perturbations are common and emergent features of galaxy assembly; in 51$\%$ of our total samples ($54\%$ TNG100-1 galaxies and $85\%$ for TNG50-1 galaxies), the addition of multipole terms to a standard elliptical power-law model is preferred by the Bayesian Information Criterion. Ignoring these structures in lens modelling introduces systematic biases in $H_0$: for perturbations of $\sim2\%$, the biases are of the order of 1--2$\%$ for $m=1$, 4--5$\%$ for $m=3$, and 3--6$\%$ for $m=4$ multipole. While the $m=4$ (boxy/discyness) mode induces a predictable directional bias, odd-order modes ($m=1,3$) introduce significant stochastic scatter. We demonstrate a degeneracy where unmodelled internal multipoles can be effectively absorbed by the external shear component. This combined with low signal-to-noise observations can create ``hidden biases'' where formally perfect fits to imaging data ($\chi_\nu^2 \approx 1$) could harbour significant cosmological errors. Our results provide an astrophysical basis for multipole priors and highlight the necessity of moving beyond rigid parametric models to achieve high-accuracy cosmography in the era of \textit{Euclid} and LSST.

**Relevance:** 2
**Novelty:** 6

---

## 32. [Simple multi-interacting fluids in SymBoltz](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.17384) <a id="link32"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2611.17384

**Authors:** David Figueruelo, Herman Sletmoen

**Abstract:** We implement a simple and general way of specifying arbitrary interactions between baryons, dark matter and dark energy in the Einstein--Boltzmann code SymBoltz. Due to the symbolic-numeric interface and approximation-free structure of SymBoltz, the model definition is remarkably compact and convenient to modify by specifying only the covariant coupling. The full equations are stiff, but stable and fast to integrate with an efficient implicit solver, particularly with the sparse and analytical ODE Jacobian generated from the symbolic equations. We first validate it using single-interaction models, comparing their background evolution and matter and CMB power spectra with those from modified versions of CLASS, finding $0.1\%$--$1\%$ agreement at most scales. Finally, we show how our framework makes it trivial to implement models with any interactions of any complexity among all three species simultaneously, which requires a more involved implementation in other Einstein--Boltzmann codes like CLASS or CAMB. Our full model is specified in only around 300 lines of code and is easy to modify for any other interaction by just specifying it in three lines.

**Relevance:** 2
**Novelty:** 6

H. Wright (Ruhr University Bochum, Faculty of Physics and Astronomy, Astronomical Institute), D. Scott (Department of Physics and Astronomy, University of British Columbia, Vancouver, BC V6T 1Z1, Canada)

**Abstract:** The European Space Agency's Euclid mission has issued its first major data release(Data Release 1, DR1), providing a transformative view of the extragalactic sky through deep photometry and near-infrared slitless spectroscopy. This paper presents the Euclid DR1 spectroscopic data set and details the evolution of the spectroscopic processing function (SPE PF) pipeline, which is responsible for the automated analysis of 1-dimensional spectra acquired by the Near-Infrared Spectro-Photometer (NISP). The SPE PF pipeline workflow comprises spectroscopic classification, redshift determination, and the systematic measurement of spectral features. Emission line fluxes are derived using two independent methodologies: direct integration (DI) and Gaussian fitting (GF). For sources exhibiting broad H$\alpha$ profiles (velocity dispersion larger than $1500$ km s$^{-1}$), a dedicated `QSO mode' is implemented to robustly deconvolve nuclear and host galaxy components. The data set was validated by cross-matching Euclid results with external spectroscopic catalogues, specifically Dark Energy Spectroscopic Instrument (DESI) DR1, and through an extensive visual inspection campaign of approximately 4000 sources within the Euclid Deep Field North (EDF-N). For high-quality samples selected in the EDF-N, we find a 98.5\% agreement between Euclid and DESI spectroscopic redshifts. This success rate increases to 98.9\% when incorporating pipeline probability and reliability estimators. (abridged)

**Novelty:** 6

## 34. [Reconstruction of Dynamical Dark Energy and Gravity from latest cosmological data](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.18558) <a id="link34"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2611.18558

**Authors:** Maria Luisa Gallavotti, Alessandra Silvestri

**Abstract:** Recent cosmological observations have renewed interest in non-standard late-time evolution. We investigate whether departures from the standard expansion history are accompanied by modifications of gravitational dynamics through a joint, non-parametric reconstruction of the effective dark energy density, $\Omega_{\rm X}(a)$, and the gravitational response functions $\mu(a)$ and $\Sigma(a)$. Combining CMB, DESI DR2 BAO, Pantheon+, and large scale structure observations, including DES Year 3 $6\times2$pt data, we perform reconstructions with and without a theory motivated correlation prior derived from stable Horndeski models. We find a persistent maximum in $\Omega_{\rm X}$ at $z\simeq0.5$--$0.7$, corresponding to a crossing of the effective phantom divide. This feature is already present in the CMB+BAO combination and remains robust when modifications to clustering and lensing are allowed. The reconstructed gravitational response exhibits suggestive enhancements in $\mu$ and $\Sigma$ over approximately the same redshift range, although these departures are less significant. Including LSS information instead favours a reduced lensing response, $\Sigma<1$, while leaving the background feature largely unchanged. An interpretation within Generalized Brans-Dicke theories illustrates how modifications to the effective gravitational coupling and a scalar-mediated fifth force may contribute to these trends. Our results indicate that current departures from $\Lambda$CDM are most significant in the expansion history, while leaving open the possibility of correlated modifications to gravitational dynamics that future Stage IV surveys can test.

**Relevance:** 2
**Novelty:** 6

## 35. [Distinguishing cosmologies with topological analysis of 21-cm hydrogen 2D brightness fields at Cosmic Dawn](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.18407) <a id="link35"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2611.18407

**Authors:** Maksym Tsizh, Anton Rudakovskyi, Vitalii Tymchyshyn

**Abstract:** The 21-cm hydrogen line brightness field is a potentially valuable source of information about astrophysical parameters during the cosmological Dark Ages and the Epoch of Reionization. However, it can also be a source of observational data for cosmologists seeking to constrain cosmological parameters. While much of the effort has been focused on constraining parameters using information across a broad frequency range, most often with the power spectrum of inhomogeneities, we investigate whether a single frequency channel, corresponding to a single redshift slice, can distinguish between cosmological models. We utilize topological data analysis to evaluate the possibility of such constraints. In particular, we use cubical complexes to perform persistent homology analysis of the 21-cm line brightness field. We generate 21-cm brightness fields of different cosmologies with the 21cmFAST package and then select snapshots at several redshifts corresponding to the Epoch of Reionization. Our goal is to find the redshift that is most suitable for highlighting the diversity between scenarios within a certain range of cosmological parameters. To compare persistent pictures built on simulations of the brightness field, we use Wasserstein distance, computed between any two persistent diagrams. We find that observations of the 21-cm field near $z=10$ have the potential to distinguish between cosmological models with different values of the normalized Hubble parameter $h_0$, the spectral index of scalar matter perturbations $n_s$, the matter density parameter $\Omega_m$, and the matter fluctuation amplitude $\sigma_8$, even in the presence of instrumental thermal noise. We perform the PERMANOVA statistical test on the matrices of Wasserstein distances to confirm this hypothesis for all considered cosmological parameters.

**Novelty:** 6

## 36. [Full-shape analysis of the Lyman-alpha forest power spectrum and bispectrum: validation on simulations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.17405) <a id="link36"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2611.17405

**Authors:** Roger de Belsunce, James M. Sullivan, Mikhail M. Ivanov

**Abstract:** The Lyman-$\alpha$ (LyA) forest of neutral hydrogen provides a large-volume, high-resolution map of cosmological fluctuations from optical spectra at high redshift. To extract more information from the currently observing Dark Energy Spectroscopic Instrument (DESI), we derive an effective field theory (EFT) model for joint full-shape analyses of the LyA forest data. It combines the one-loop power spectrum of the LyA forest (FF), and of its cross-correlation with dark-matter halos, used as proxies for high-redshift quasars (FQ), with the tree-level bispectrum of the LyA forest (FFF). Importantly, the bispectrum breaks the LyA-specific degeneracy between the mean flux and the amplitude of the power spectrum. We validate our theory framework on idealized large-volume LyA forest mocks painted onto the AbacusSummit N-body simulation suite at redshift z=2.5. Fitting scales up to $k_\mathrm{max}^{FF\,(FQ)}=0.6\,(0.3)\,h\,\mathrm{Mpc}^{-1}$ in the power spectrum and $k_\mathrm{max}^{FFF}=0.1\,h\,\mathrm{Mpc}^{-1}$ in the bispectrum, our full-shape analysis yields unbiased constraints on the cosmological parameters in simulations. For a fully sampled flux field in a simulated volume of $V=8\,h^{-3}\,{\rm Gpc}^3$, we measure the Hubble constant, $H_0$, to $0.6\%$, the matter density, $\Omega_{\rm m}$, to $1.6\%$ and the amplitude of matter clustering, $\sigma_8$, to $3.5\%$. Adding the bispectrum improves the constraints on $\sigma_8$ by $28\%$. We also demonstrate that the inclusion of the quasar one-loop power spectrum (QQ) and tree-level bispectrum (QQQ) further improves cosmological constraints by $\approx 35\%$ using $k_\mathrm{max}^{QQ,\,(QQQ)}=0.2,\,(0.08)\,h\,\mathrm{Mpc}^{-1}$. Our theory framework enables full-shape analyses of high-redshift data from DESI and its successor DESI-II, enhancing knowledge of the growth of structure of the Universe during matter-domination.

**Novelty:** 6

## 37. [The Gravitational Wave Foreground from Galactic Cataclysmic Variables in LISA](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.17421) <a id="link37"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2611.17421

**Authors:** Levi Schult, Alexander Criswell, Stephen R. Taylor, Katelyn Breivik

**Abstract:** Electromagnetic observations have been the primary medium for studying cataclysmic variable (CV) systems, capitalizing on the invaluable opportunity they present to study binary stellar evolution and accretion. The launch of the Laser Interferometer Space Antenna (LISA) mission, however, will enable a multi-messenger perspective of the population. We extend previous work to explore the gravitational wave (GW) spectra sourced by a local and Galactic population of CVs. Using the Bayesian LISA Inference Package (BLIP), we simulate LISA data containing these CV populations to understand their impact and our ability to jointly model CV and double white dwarf (DWD) GW spectra. While we find that a local CV population has negligible effect on the LISA datastream, a Galactic CV population will have a signal-to-noise ratio of approximately 76, depending on the characteristics of the DWD population present, and will bias existing DWD spectral models if ignored. Current approaches struggle to accurately recover both populations' respective spectra, calling for the creation of multi-component spectral models that include GW contributions from both CVs and DWDs. These results point toward a promising future for the study of CVs with LISA.

**Relevance:** 2
**Novelty:** 5

## 38. [Counter-rotating Ionized and Neutral Gas in a Star-forming Elliptical Galaxy](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.17740) <a id="link38"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2611.17740

**Authors:** Pralay Biswas, Yogesh Wadadekar, Omkar Bait, Sushma Kurapati

**Abstract:** In early-type galaxies, gas that is kinematically misaligned with the stars generally rotates together across all gas phases, implying a single external origin. Using upgraded Giant Metrewave Radio Telescope (uGMRT) 21 cm imaging and SDSS-MaNGA spatially resolved spectroscopy, we studied MaNGA 10214-1902, a compact ($R_e = 0.78$ kpc), star-forming elliptical galaxy with stellar mass $\log M_*/M_{\odot} = 9.90$, in which the ionized gas counter-rotates with respect to the stars. It hosts $\log(M_{\rm HI}/M_{\odot}) = 9.65$ in a 45 kpc HI disk that co-rotates with the stars and has a central column-density depression. Inside this depression, the ionized gas ($r \lesssim 2$ kpc) rotates in the opposite sense to both the stars ($\Delta{\rm PA} = 152^\circ \pm 20^\circ$) and the HI that surrounds it ($\Delta{\rm PA} = 179^\circ \pm 10^\circ$). HI co-spatial with the ionized gas is not detected, so a fainter inner HI component with a different orientation cannot be ruled out. This central gas is star-forming in the [SII] and [OI] diagnostic diagrams and has EW(HI) $>6~\mathrm{\mathring{A}}$ at the center. The 1.4 GHz radio-continuum star formation rate agrees with the spectral energy distribution based value, showing no evidence of a dominant active galactic nucleus. The stellar population at the center is younger than the outskirts and has the lowest D$_{\rm n}$4000. These results are consistent with the acquisition of gas with opposite angular momentum into the center of a pre-existing HI disk that co-rotates with the stars, which can fuel the most recent central star formation.

**Relevance:** 2
**Novelty:** 5

## 39. [PyVesta: A new Versatile Echelle Spectra exTraction and Analysis pipeline](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.18154) <a id="link39"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2611.18154

**Authors:** Lukas Stock, Andreas Schrimpf

**Abstract:** We present PyVesta, an automatic data reduction software for fiber fed echelle spectrographs. PyVesta is designed to be used with different instruments, both high resolution spectrographs such as HARPS or FEROS as well as inexpensive off-the-shelf spectrographs like the Shelyak eShel II. Special additions were implemented to improve the stability and resolution of inexpensive instruments especially. PyVesta provides tools for all extraction steps from raw CCD images to continuum normalized spectra, e.g., CCD image corrections, spectra extraction, wavelength calibration, and continuum normalization. Our software is based on the software package CERES and was ported to Python 3, revised, and supplemented.

**Relevance:** 2

## 40. [A New Galactic Close Binary Supersoft Source Candidate](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.18441) <a id="link40"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2611.18441

**Authors:** Gael R. C. Barreteau, Marcos P. Diaz, Paulo E. Stecchini, Natália Palivanas, Alexandre S. Oliveira, Larissa Mazzero, Flavio D'Amico, Francisco Jablonski

**Abstract:** The identification of a new galactic Close Binary Supersoft Source (CBSS) candidate, whose main ID is ZTF J043609.49+364057.7, is presented on the basis of the analysis of its optical spectrum, light curve and Spectral Energy Distribution (SED). During a survey designed to find candidates to the aforementioned class independently of X-ray emissions, this object's optical spectrum was found to exhibit the typical emission features expected for a CBSS. The available photometric data show a quasi-sinusoidal modulation, with a period of 5.42 h, compatible with the orbital modulation of the system. Although there is no current identification of X-ray emission from this source, its SED displays a highly absorbed bright FUV component, given the estimated E(B - V) ~ 0.7 mag.

## 41. [Composition-dependent ultralight dark matter detection with a tetrahedral superconducting gravity gradiometer](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.17345) <a id="link41"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2611.17345

**Authors:** John J. Oh, Ho Jung Paik

**Abstract:** Tetrahedral superconducting gravity gradiometers designed as full-tensor gravitational-wave detectors, such as the ground-based mSOGRO design and the proposed lunar LANGO observatory, reconstruct the acceleration-gradient tensor from four test masses. We show that giving the four test masses two compositions in a $2+2$ arrangement adds a composition-dependent ultralight dark matter (ULDM) channel to that same tensor readout, with no separate dark-matter sensor and no dark-matter-specific readout. A coherently oscillating scalar accelerates each mass in proportion to its dilaton charge, and the tensor reconstruction leaves the charges only in a mean, rejected with the common mode, and a single vector $\qv$ proportional to the charge contrast $\Delta\abaralpha_{AB}$ between the two materials. The response is the rank-one tensor $\K_{\ULDM}(t)=\gv_\phi(t)\otimes\qv$, whose amplitude is set by the halo, independent of $m_\phi$. The axis of $\qv$ is fixed in advance by the material assignment, so the response obeys the null condition $\K_{\ULDM}(\mathbf{I}-\Pq)=0$ at every instant, for any $\gv_\phi$ including a virialized field with a stochastic gradient---an exact tensor discriminator against gravitational-wave signals; for a single composition $\qv=0$. With the gravitational-wave noise model, the coupling sensitivity for copper and lead test masses reaches $d_{\hat m}-d_g\simeq3.4\times10^{-8}$ for the baseline instrument and $4.9\times10^{-10}$ with upgraded accelerometers. The rank-one factorization and the null condition are summarized in a companion Letter; the present paper gives the derivations and the detector-level analysis.

**Relevance:** 2
**Novelty:** 5
