## 42. [Astrometric microlensing by isolated black holes in the Roman Galactic Bulge Time Domain Survey](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.17410) <a id="link42"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2611.17410

**Authors:** Zofia Kaczmarek, Peter McGill

**Abstract:** Most of the hundred million stellar-mass black holes (BHs) expected in the Milky Way are predicted to be isolated. Characterizing this BH population will provide insights into stellar evolution and supernova explosion physics. The Nancy Grace Roman Space Telescope Galactic Bulge Time Domain Survey (GBTDS) will enable the detection of isolated BHs through astrometric microlensing, apparent stellar position shifts caused by deflection of distant starlight. We combine a Galactic model, stellar population synthesis model, BH initial--final mass relation, and kick velocity distribution to simulate events caused by isolated BH lenses. We assess the expected yield, sensitivity, characterization potential, and signal characteristics of these events to inform GBTDS search strategies. We predict $487 \pm 37$ isolated-BH microlensing events with detectable astrometric signals, 82% of which have impact parameters beyond 2 Einstein radii and consequently have small or undetectable photometric signals. Standard astrometric fits yield unit weight errors > 1.2 for 18% and > 2 for 2% of these events. Sensitivity peaks for 5-6$M_\odot$ BHs and is limited by the five-year GBTDS baseline. Weak microlensing parallax constraints limit BH mass precision, but events with Einstein timescales > 100 days and angular Einstein radii > 1.2 mas have high BH lens probabilities within our Galactic model. Wide-separation astrometric microlensing offers an independent discovery channel for isolated BHs beyond photometrically selected events. Realizing this opportunity requires dedicated multi-season GBTDS searches, joint astrometric and photometric modeling, and accounting for other astrometric variability. Although precise individual masses are challenging for these events, probabilistic lens classification could lead to Galactic abundance constraints on the population of isolated BHs.

## 43. [Symbolic Regression Fitting for Roche Lobe Radius](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.17997) <a id="link43"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2611.17997

**Authors:** Ye Ma, Xiaodong Li, Bo Ma, Zhanwen Han, Cong Yu

**Abstract:** We present a compact approximation to the dimensionless, volume-equivalent Roche lobe radius $R_{\rm L}/a$, obtained by applying symbolic regression to radii calculated from the full Roche potential. Over $10^{-5}\leq q\leq10^5$, the expression reduces the root mean square error from $1.97\times10^{-3}$ for Eggleton's formula to $5.95\times10^{-4}$, while its maximum relative error is $1.39\%$, compared with Eggleton's $0.83\%$. Calculations at additional mass ratios give a mean relative error of $0.331\%$. The formula is monotonic, recovers the $q^{\frac{1}{3}}$ scaling at small mass ratio, and approaches a large-mass-ratio limit within $0.036\%$ of the limiting Roche model. In a NumPy float64 benchmark of $10^7$ direct formula evaluations, the SR expression takes $0.217$~s compared with $0.450$~s for Eggleton, a speed-up of $2.08$. These results quantify the accuracy and evaluation cost of a compact algebraic alternative for binary-system calculations.

## 44. [The Eclipsing $\delta$ Scuti Binary HIP 7666: Absolute Dimensions and Pulsation Properties](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.17590) <a id="link44"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2611.17590

**Authors:** Jae Woo Lee, Kyeongsoo Hong, Min-Ji Jeong, Pakakaew Rittipruk, Jang-Ho Park

**Abstract:** We present a comprehensive analysis of the pulsating eclipsing binary HIP 7666 (TIC 308953703), containing a $\delta$ Sct-type component, based on 120-s TESS photometry and 63 newly acquired high-resolution echelle spectra. While previous orbital solutions were limited by sparse spectroscopic sampling, our extensive radial velocity (RV) measurements enable a robust determination of the system's physical parameters. The primary has an effective temperature of $T_{\rm eff,A}$ = 7376$\pm$72 K and a projected rotational velocity of $v_{\rm A}\sin i$ = 56.6$\pm$5.3 km s$^{-1}$. Joint modeling of the light and RV curves shows that HIP 7666 is a detached system in a circular orbit, with Roche-lobe filling factors of $f_{\rm A}$ = 57 \% and $f_{\rm B}$ = 44 \%. The masses and radii are determined to better than 2 \% precision: $M_{\rm A}$ = 1.822$\pm$0.029 $M_\odot$, $R_{\rm A}$ = 2.154$\pm$0.017 $R_\odot$ for the primary, and $M_{\rm B}$ = 1.246$\pm$0.020 $M_\odot$, $R_{\rm B}$ = 1.170$\pm$0.015 $R_\odot$ for the secondary. The binary-based distance (316$\pm$8 pc) agrees well with the Gaia DR3 parallax-based distance (313$\pm$1 pc), validating the derived system parameters. Frequency analysis of the light-curve residuals reveals 15 significant signals, predominantly in the $\delta$ Sct pulsation frequency range. PARSEC evolutionary tracks indicate an age of $\sim$0.9 Gyr and a supersolar metallicity ($Z=0.03$), with the primary on the main sequence and the secondary located close to the zero-age main sequence. Galactic kinematics place HIP 7666 in the thin disk.

## 45. [Constraining Accretion Regimes of Little Red Dots from Their Variability](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.18153) <a id="link45"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2611.18153

**Authors:** Indu K. Dihingia

**Abstract:** The ``little red dots'' (LRDs) are photometrically and spectroscopically non-variable on rest-frame baselines of months, with a $5.9\sigma$ deficit relative to ordinary active galactic nuclei (AGNs). If we consider LRDs to be accreting black holes, this non-varying could be a clue about the nature of the accretion. Here, we present a general semi-analytic model of variability from super-Eddington (slim) accretion disks. We consider three mechanisms that play crucial roles in the observed variability, viz., advective attenuation, photospheric averaging, and photon diffusion filter. With this, above $\mdot \sim$ a few, the fractional rms collapses steeply with $\mdot$. Comparing with JWST monitoring constraints, we find that the non-detection of LRD variability requires $\mdot \gtrsim 30$ for $M_{\rm BH}\sim10^{7}\,M_\odot$ on a 0.5\,yr rest-frame baseline; a Monte Carlo forward model of the TWINKLE non-detections of 18 LRDs sharpens this to $\mdot\gtrsim60$ at 95\% confidence. On current baselines, these non-detections remain formally degenerate with a frozen sub-Eddington disk ($\mdot\lesssim0.1$). Longer monitoring will separate the two regimes as the frozen-disk variability grows with baseline, while the super-Eddington collapse does not. The same framework predicts strongly suppressed X-ray variability, testable with future X-ray observatories.

## 46. [Polarization of strongly lensed gravitational waves as a probe of the lens-induced orientations of electromagnetic counterparts](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.17637) <a id="link46"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2611.17637

**Authors:** Kyungmin Kim

**Abstract:** Strong gravitational lensing is a well-established phenomenon in electromagnetic (EM) observations, where the lensed images and arcs are resolved on the two-dimensional sky and constrain the mass distribution of the lens. Strongly lensed gravitational waves (GWs) are also expected to be detected routinely by next-generation detectors, but their multiple images are recorded only as one-dimensional time series without positions. Among them, lensed binary neutron star mergers can be accompanied by EM counterparts with resolvable structures, such as relativistic jets. In EM lensing, the lens-induced change in the apparent orientation of such a structure can be measured only relative to other images or statistically, because its intrinsic orientation is not directly observable. In this work, we show that the GW polarization, which the lens leaves unchanged, provides this missing reference, i.e., a compass, so that the orientation offset of each image can be measured in absolute terms. We place this orientation layer on the EM image plane together with image positions from the lensed host arcs and Fermat-potential heights from GW time delays and magnifications. Using a mock population of such mergers observable by the Einstein Telescope and Cosmic Explorer, we find that the predicted image positions and arrival times are calibrated once the detection selection is included. The lens-induced orientation offsets reach tens of degrees and are detectable in up to a third of the events, at viewing angles above about $40^\circ$ for quadrupole-dominated signals.

## 47. [General $\delta N$ formalism in Single Field Inflation and Graph Theory](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.17978) <a id="link47"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2611.17978

**Authors:** Amin Nassiri-Rad, Hassan Firouzjahi

**Abstract:** We study higher order correlation functions in single field inflation using the $\delta N$ formalism and methods from graph theory. By representing connected tree level contributions as graphs, we systematically classify their independent topologies, momentum shapes and amplitudes. We show that the amplitudes of arbitrary trees factorize into products of primary amplitudes, providing a general framework for relations among higher order correlators, including the Suyama-Yamaguchi equality. As the number of external legs increases, the number of distinct tree topologies grows exponentially, whereas the number of algebraically distinct amplitude products grows considerably more slowly. As an application, we consider ultra-slow-roll inflation followed by a transition to a slow-roll attractor. For transitions without a potential step, we derive an explicit all-order expression for the vertex amplitudes and establish their positivity and dependence on the sharpness of the transition. When a potential step is included, we prove that all nonlinear vertex amplitudes remain positive for upward steps, whereas downward steps allow cancellations at specific parameter values. These cancellations can eliminate entire families of tree contributions, including configurations in which the leading local bispectrum and trispectrum vanish simultaneously while higher order correlations remain nonzero.

## 48. [A Multiscale Analysis of an Interplanetary Shock from IMAP, SOLAR-1, and the L1 Constellation to Earth](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.18314) <a id="link48"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2611.18314

**Authors:** Savvas Raptis, Drew L. Turner, Domenico Trotta, Vassilis Angelopoulos, James L. Burch, Dibyendu Chakrabarty, Eric Christian, Christina M. S. Cohen, Manuel E. Cuesta, Maher A. Dayeh, Robert W. Ebert, Hameedullah A. Farooki, Joe Giacalone, Matina Gkioulidou, Timothy Horbury, Immanuel C. Jebaraj, David J. McComas, Connor O'Brien, Srikar Paavan Tadepalli, Jamie S. Rankin, K. Sankarasubramanian, Nathan Schwadron, Bishwas L. Shrestha, Adam Szabo, Roy Torbert, Dimitrios Vassiliadis, Sarah Vines, Hanying Wei, Lynn B. Wilson III, Gary P. Zank, Lingling Zhao

**Abstract:** Collisionless shocks are primary sites of particle acceleration throughout the Universe. Transient, mesoscale structures forming around these shocks influence their energy conversion efficiency, yet remain poorly observed at interplanetary (IP) shocks: spacecraft sample the shock transition briefly, and single-point measurements cannot disentangle temporal evolution from spatial structure. Here, we examine a fast, CME-driven IP shock of 2026 January 19, observed by IMAP, SOLAR-1, Wind, ACE, DSCOVR, and Aditya-L1 at L1 and by ARTEMIS and MMS near Earth, to determine its spatial and temporal structure. At MHD scales, a pre-existing solar wind flux tube is overtaken by the shock as it crosses the L1 fleet. This inflates the apparent magnetic compression ratio far beyond the ideal strong-shock limit of 4, showing that upstream and downstream intervals can sample different plasma and bias single-spacecraft estimates. At kinetic scales, IMAP and SOLAR-1, separated by only $\sim$7 $R_E$ ($\sim$340 ion inertial lengths), sample the same foreshock yet record localized structures that differ measurably; these are the first candidate signatures of large-scale foreshock transients at an IP shock. Further upstream, shocklets appear hundreds of $R_E$ ahead of the shock, consistent with steepening of ambient solar wind waves. Near Earth, the shock-to-flux-tube arrival lag increases to approximately six minutes, corresponding to a nominal sheath extension of $\sim$50 $R_E$, indicating sheath expansion over $\sim$200 $R_E$ of propagation. Together, these results show that the IP shock, its foreshock, and its sheath evolve across multiple scales from L1 to Earth, underscoring the need for multipoint measurements to resolve the underlying processes.

**Relevance:** 1
**Novelty:** 6

## 49. [Microscopic Realizability of Non-unique Bubble Hydrodynamics](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.17402) <a id="link49"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2611.17402

**Authors:** Giulio Barni, Eric Madge

**Abstract:** First-order phase transitions drive fluid motion that induces energy transport and gravitational-wave production. The flows are usually classified in terms of the wall velocity and the transition strength. We show that this description fails when the two phases have unequal sound speeds $c_\pm$. New mixed-hybrid profiles may appear, and for a strong hierarchy, $c_+\gg c_-$, where the phase behind the wall is much softer, heating or cooling ahead of the wall allow for the coexistence of distinct stationary flows at the same wall velocity and transition strength. This ambiguity is removed once thermodynamic normalization and entropy production are specified. We identify microscopic settings that realize these sound-speed hierarchies. In thermal plasmas it requires exponentially large mass-to-temperature ratios, whereas at finite density a conserved charge removes this obstruction, making dense matter its natural arena.

**Novelty:** 6

## 50. [JWST/NIRISS-SOSS Spectroscopic Variability and Auroral Modelling of Warm Ultracool Dwarf LSR J1835+3259](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.18246) <a id="link50"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2611.18246

**Authors:** Eleanor Pike, Stuart Littlefair, J. Sebastian Pineda, Melodie Kao, Fei Wang, Ben Burningham, David García-Álvarez, Gregg Hallinan, Caroline Morley, John E. Gizis, John D. Nichols

**Abstract:** Auroral variability in ultracool dwarfs (UCDs) has been proposed as a key source of optical and infrared modulation, yet its physical origin remains poorly understood. We present JWST Near Infrared Imager and Slitless Spectrograph/Single Object Slitless Spectroscopy (NIRISS/SOSS) time-series spectroscopy of the M8.5 UCD LSR J1835+3259. These observations probe the object's wavelength-dependent rotational variability, providing insight into the atmospheric processes underlying its infrared behaviour. Additional GTC/MEGARA optical spectra reveal that its H$\alpha$ emission has declined markedly since earlier epochs, suggesting a reduced activity state during the JWST observations. We detect no emission from H$_3^+$ or absorption by hydronium in the NIRISS spectra. We use principal component analysis to identify two distinct modes of spectral variability: one dominated by FeH modulation near 1 $\mu$m and another contributing broadband variability across the H and K bands. We find that no model atmosphere grid provides a reasonable fit to the average NIRISS spectrum. However, a blended model comprising an M-dwarf photosphere and a cooler blackbody component provides an improved fit to the mean spectrum, the broadband spectral energy distribution and the NIRISS broadband variability. By adding perturbations to PICASO model grids, we demonstrate that a combination of atmospheric heating and non-equilibrium chemistry can successfully mimic a blend of unperturbed photosphere and a blackbody, and that the presence of these effects may explain the unusual spectrum and variability of LSR J1835+3259.

## 51. [Gravitational-wave hierarchical inference from population samples](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.17428) <a id="link51"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2611.17428

**Authors:** Federico De Santi, Davide Gerosa, Matthew Mould

**Abstract:** Standard approaches to gravitational-wave population inference require evaluating the probability density of the population model. While appropriate for models composed of exact functional forms, this approach is not compatible with state-of-the-art astrophysical predictions from population-synthesis codes, which simulate samples from astrophysical models whose densities are unknown. We present an approach that solely requires samples and never density evaluations of the targeted population model. Key technical ingredients include efficient surrogates for the single-event likelihoods and detection probability, together with an efficient sampling strategy. Using detections up to GWTC-5.0, we validate the method against standard inference with a parametric model, showcase its flexibility on a simulated population including hierarchical mergers, and present a realistic application to a grid of astrophysical population-synthesis simulations with five formation channels. This work paves the way toward a direct comparison of astrophysical models and gravitational-wave data.

## 52. [Euclid DR1 Legacy Science on Ultracool Dwarfs: New URLD candidates in Euclid and SPHEREx](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.17519) <a id="link52"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2611.17519

**Authors:** N. Lodieu (IAC, La Laguna, Tenerife, Spain, ULL, La Laguna, Tenerife, Spain), M. Žerjal (IAC, La Laguna, Tenerife, Spain, ULL, La Laguna, Tenerife, Spain), N. Vitas (IAC, La Laguna, Tenerife, Spain, ULL, La Laguna, Tenerife, Spain), C. Domínguez Tagle (IAC, La Laguna, Tenerife, Spain, ULL, La Laguna, Tenerife, Spain), E. L. Martín (IAC, La Laguna, Tenerife, Spain, ULL, La Laguna, Tenerife, Spain), J. -Y. Zhang (University of Western Ontario, Canada), S. Tsilia (IAC, La Laguna, Tenerife, Spain, ULL, La Laguna, Tenerife, Spain)

**Abstract:** Unusually red L dwarfs (URLDs) probe extreme atmospheric conditions in the substellar regime, where enhanced dust opacity, cloud structure, and surface gravity effects challenge standard brown dwarf models. As free-floating analogues of young giant exoplanets, they provide key benchmarks for interpreting low-resolution infrared spectra from current and future wide-field space missions.   The goal is to identify new red L dwarf candidates both photometrically and spectroscopically in the first data release (DR1) of the Euclid space mission. The area covered is about 1,900 and 50 square degrees in the wide and deep survey, respectively.   We used colour cuts in multiple colour-colour diagrams combined with point-source and quality criteria to identify candidate red L dwarfs in both the Euclid wide and deep survey fields. We cross-matched photometric candidates with the SPHEREx mission and Euclid red grism spectra to confirm spectroscopic properties and evaluate bolometric magnitudes and colours.   We identified 1202 and 38 red L dwarf candidates in the Euclid wide and deep surveys, respectively. Spectroscopic follow-up and archival cross-matching confirm a substantial new population of red L dwarfs. Distance-independent diagnostics show that these objects occupy distinct loci characterised by enhanced condensate opacity and significant redness.   Euclid vastly expands the census of unusually red L dwarfs. While preliminary spectral classifications suggest many of these new candidates are intermediate-age (Pleiades-like) objects, a subset exhibits extremely red signatures indicative of very young, low-gravity (<=10 Myr) atmospheres, providing vital benchmarks for young exoplanet research.

## 53. [Receiving Exoplanet Messages with Gravitational-wave Carriers: The Gravitational-wave Radial Velocity Method](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.17571) <a id="link53"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2611.17571

**Authors:** Casey McGrath

**Abstract:** Gravitational-wave (GW) radial velocity (RV) may offer a new method for detecting exoplanets, one that would primarily target planets orbiting GW-emitting, ultracompact, post-main-sequence stellar objects such as white dwarfs, neutron stars, and black holes. In this work we show that while similar to electromagnetic-based RV, this GW-based method depends on the ''phase modulation index'' (PMI), a parameter that is more sensitive to high-mass, long-period exoplanets. We investigate the potential of GW RV for GW sources in both the mid-frequency spectrum (where the upcoming LISA mission will operate) and the high-frequency spectrum (where the current LIGO-Virgo-KAGRA (LVK) observatories operate). Remarkably, we find that the PMI increases by a factor of $\sim \mathrm{O}\left(10^4 - 10^6\right)$ going from the mid-frequency to the high-frequency GW spectrum, which results in a greater redistribution of observed power due to the exoplanet's presence. By analyzing the power content of the observed GW signal, we derive analytic expressions to predict the GW carrier frequency power loss due to the exoplanet and the signal-to-noise ratio of the GW source and the exoplanet. We also explore the effects of the additional Doppler effect caused by the motion of the GW observatory and the potential for GW source parameter bias introduced by the presence of an undetected exoplanet. All of this helps us better understand where the GW RV method may work best in the exoplanet mass-period discovery space.

## 54. [HydróDex: gotta match 'em all!](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.17411) <a id="link54"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2611.17411

**Authors:** Giulio Barni, Eric Madge

**Abstract:** We present a comprehensive hydrodynamic classification of stationary fluid profiles in first-order phase transitions within the constant-sound-speed model, a physically motivated extension of the standard bag model that allows the sound speeds in front of and behind the wall, $c_+$ and $c_-$, to differ. Solving the matching conditions at the bubble wall and evolving the profile in the surrounding plasma, we systematically identify and classify all the possible stationary solutions. The classification recovers all previously known direct and inverse detonation, deflagration, and hybrid solutions, while revealing a new class of stationary configurations, termed mixed hybrids, featuring rarefaction waves on both sides of the wall and oppositely directed fluid flows. For a sufficiently pronounced hierarchy $c_+ > c_-$, the classification in terms of the wall velocity $\xi_w$ and the asymptotic strength parameter $\alpha_N$ further exhibits degenerate regions in parameter space, where multiple distinct stationary profiles correspond to the same physical inputs $(\xi_w,\alpha_N)$. This non-uniqueness arises as the parametrization discards thermodynamic information and is resolved once the underlying microphysics is specified. We also compute the kinetic-energy budget and the associated acoustic gravitational-wave spectra within the sound-shell model, finding that mixed-hybrid solutions, despite their oppositely directed flows, introduce no additional spectral feature, and their spectra can be closely mimicked by ordinary direct or inverse hybrids with very different fluid profiles. Finally, we release Hydr\'oDex, a public code that implements the complete hydrodynamic classification, fluid-profile construction, kinetic-energy budget, and gravitational-wave calculation.

## 55. [Rank-One Ultralight Dark Matter Response in a Full-Tensor Gravity Gradiometer](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.17344) <a id="link55"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2611.17344

**Authors:** John J. Oh, Ho Jung Paik

**Abstract:** A full-tensor superconducting gravity gradiometer whose four test masses carry two compositions in a $2+2$ pattern acquires a composition-dependent channel for dilaton-coupled ultralight dark matter. The reconstructed response is the rank-one tensor $\K=\gv_\phi(t)\otimes\qv$, with the axis of $\qv$ fixed in advance by the material assignment, and obeys the parameter-free null condition $\K(\mathbf{I}-\Pq)=0$, which no gravity gradient or platform rotation alone can satisfy; it vanishes for a single composition. For copper and lead, the sensitivity reaches $d_{\hat m}-d_g\simeq3.4\times10^{-8}$, and $4.9\times10^{-10}$ with upgraded accelerometers.

## 56. [Suprathermal bulk viscosity in binary neutron star mergers](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.18146) <a id="link56"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2611.18146

**Authors:** Pranjal Tambe, Debarati Chatterjee

**Abstract:** Numerical relativity simulations of binary neutron star mergers and core-collapse supernovae indicate that the neutron star matter can be driven far from equilibrium due to large amplitude density oscillations, with frequency in the kHz range. Urca processes in neutron star matter try to restore the system towards equilibrium providing strong bulk viscous damping of these density oscillations. Standard bulk viscosity calculations typically assume small amplitude density oscillations, such that the deviation from chemical equilibrium $\mu_{\Delta}$ is less than the temperature $T$. In this work we compute the complete amplitude dependent bulk viscosity, taking into account, for the first time, thermal blurring effects and collisional broadening of in-medium nucleons. Our results show that the non-linear effects in bulk viscosity start to become important when the oscillation amplitude is about $10$ percent of the background density. In this regime the bulk viscosity can increase by three to five orders of magnitude. These results have important consequences for simulations of binary neutron star mergers as well as their multi-messenger observations.

Wilde (Institut de Ciències del Cosmos), A. H. Wright (Ruhr University Bochum, Faculty of Physics and Astronomy, Astronomical Institute), D. Scott (Department of Physics and Astronomy, University of British Columbia, Vancouver, BC V6T 1Z1, Canada)

**Abstract:** Strong gravitational lens modelling requires the lens light, lensed source light, and contaminants to be separated in each image before mass modelling codes can fit the system. Within Euclid this separation has so far been done by hand, which does not scale to the more than 15,000 candidates in the Euclid Lens Identification Pipeline and Scientific Exploration (ECLIPSE) catalogue of Data Release 1 (DR1). We present AstroVink-TREX , a neural network that performs this separation automatically and predicts three flux maps, in a standard format directly usable by mass-modelling pipelines. The network uses a hybrid architecture, pairing a vision transformer encoder reused from the lens detection network with a parallel convolutional branch. A custom loss function penalises both flux and structural error, while allowing independent weighting per component. From the network's predictions we also derive the modelling centre, fitting region, conjugate points, and binary maps, providing all the inputs that a modelling pipeline needs to run without manual setup. We evaluate the efficiency of the network on DR1 systems against existing fits and through expert validation. Across approximately 3000 inspected systems, taking 75% agreement between inspectors, just 39 lens models fail because of a segmentation error. Following this validation, the network provides the component separation and modelling setup for every system modelled in DR1. With automated segmentation, lens modelling is no longer limited by manual preprocessing and can now operate at the same scale as lens discovery.

**Relevance:** 1
**Novelty:** 6

## 58. [Exoplanets are predominately aligned with exo-Kuiper Belts](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.17423) <a id="link58"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2611.17423

**Authors:** Jingwen Zhang, Maxwell A. Millar-Blanchaer, Brendan P. Bowler, Tim D. Pearce, Marvin Morgan, William Thompson, Dori Blakely

**Abstract:** The architectures of planetary systems encode their formation and dynamical histories. In the Solar System, the near alignment of planetary orbits, the Kuiper Belt, and the Sun's equator supports planet formation in a rotating disk of gas and dust. However, some exoplanet systems show planets on orbits misaligned with their host stars or other planets, raising the question of whether misalignments are primordial or produced by post-formation dynamics. We present the first population-level constraints on the mutual inclinations of 32 planets in 16 systems relative to debris disks. We find that planets across orbital distances from 0.1 to 100 AU are predominantly aligned with the debris disks within 10 deg (30/32 planets). The alignment holds across systems spanning tens of million years to a few gigayears in age. These results indicate that the aligned architecture of the Solar System is representative of the birth conditions of exoplanet systems.

**Relevance:** 1
**Novelty:** 6

Vinciguerra (Aix-Marseille Université, CNRS, CNES, LAM, Marseille, France), M. von Wietersheim-Kramsta (Department of Physics, Institute for Computational Cosmology, Durham University, South Road, Durham, DH1 3LE, UK, Department of Physics, Centre for Extragalactic Astronomy, Durham University, South Road, Durham, DH1 3LE, UK), N. A. Walton (Institute of Astronomy, University of Cambridge, Madingley Road, Cambridge CB3 0HA, UK), L. Wang (SRON Netherlands Institute for Space Research, Landleven 12, 9747 AD, Groningen, The Netherlands, Kapteyn Astronomical Institute, University of Groningen, PO Box 800, 9700 AV Groningen, The Netherlands), A. H. Wright (Ruhr University Bochum, Faculty of Physics and Astronomy, Astronomical Institute), H. W. Yeung (Institute for Astronomy, University of Edinburgh, Royal Observatory, Blackford Hill, Edinburgh EH9 3HJ, UK), D. Scott (Department of Physics and Astronomy, University of British Columbia, Vancouver, BC V6T 1Z1, Canada)

**Abstract:** The ESA satellite mission \Euclid was launched in July 2023 and started its regular observing mode in February 2024. The data from the \Euclid instruments Visible Camera (VIS) and the Near IR Spectrometer and Photometer (NISP) are complemented by ground-based imaging data in the $u$, $g$ $r$, $i$, and $z$ bands to enable the determination of photometric redshifts and other galaxy properties. This paper describes the MERge processing function (MER PF) that has been developed and run for the Euclid Data Release 1 (DR1). The aim of the MER PF is to detect objects in the \Euclid imaging data, measure their properties, and combine them into a single multi-wavelength catalogue. In this paper we illustrate the design of the MER PF and introduce the different photometric methods applied to the data, which are Kron total flux, aperture photometry on Point Spread Functions (PSF) -matched images, template fitting photometry, and S\'ersic fitting photometry. We also present the various morphological measurements and quality flags that improve the scientific content of the output catalogues and better characterize the detected sources. We illustrate the processing and measurements that help to validate the MER PF results, which is an essential step to qualify the output for the public DR1 release. We discuss the global assessment of the photometric homogeneity and provide an internal comparison of our processing results. In addition, we give an extensive overview of the DR1 output data and the various processing scenarios

## 60. [Discovery of a Short-Duration Fast X-ray Transient Associated with a Short Gamma-Ray Burst](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.18210) <a id="link60"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2611.18210

**Authors:** Yi-Han Iris Yin, Run-Duo Liang, Hui Sun, Jun Yang, Haiwu Pan, Yong-Feng Huang, Jirong Mao, Bin-Bin Zhang, Cui-Yuan Dai, Xiang-Yu Wang, Weimin Yuan, Bing Zhang

**Abstract:** Extragalactic fast X-ray transients (EFXTs) are an emerging population of high-energy transients with diverse physical origins. Only a few observed EFXTs are associated with compact object mergers, and the known events generally display X-ray emission lasting much longer than their associated short gamma-ray bursts (GRBs). Here we report EP260411a, an $\sim8$-s EFXT discovered in archival Einstein Probe Wide-field X-ray Telescope (EP-WXT) observations through a time-targeted search for X-ray counterparts of short GRBs. This fast X-ray transient is temporally coincident with short GRB 260411A, with both the pulse width and peak time showing clear anticorrelations with the characteristic photon energy across the gamma-ray and soft X-ray bands. Joint EP-WXT and Fermi-GBM spectral fitting further shows that the broadband emission can be described by a cutoff power-law model with an additional low-energy spectral break, suggesting a common origin for the single-pulse X-ray and gamma-ray emission. The resulting properties place the event within the Type I GRB region in the $E_{\rm p,z}$-$E_{\rm iso}$ plane, supporting its classification as a compact object merger event. Unlike previously known merger-driven Type I EFXTs with long-lasting X-ray emission, EP260411a reveals that the Type I EFXT population can also extend to the short timescale of a few seconds. Its combination of short duration and hard spectrum occupies a sparsely explored region of the EFXT parameter space, providing a new observational link between short-duration EFXTs and compact object mergers.

**Relevance:** 1
**Novelty:** 6

## 61. [Many-body Response to Spin-Independent and Spin-Dependent Dark Matter Scattering in a Superfluid Helium-3 Target](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.17432) <a id="link61"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2611.17432

**Authors:** A. Ting, N. Darvishi, S. Autti, L. Bloomfield, A. Casey, N. Eng, P. Franchini, R. P. Haley, P. J. Heikkinen, A. Jennings, A. Kemp, E. Leason, J. March-Russell, J. Monroe, D. Münstermann, M. T. Noble, J. R. Prance, X. Rojas, T. Salmon, J. Saunders, J. Smirnov, R. Smith, M. D. Thompson, A. Thomson, V. Tsepelin, S. M. West, L. Whitehead, D. E. Zmeev

**Abstract:** The QUEST-DMC experiment employs an ultra-low-temperature superfluid $^3$He target in an effort to detect sub-GeV dark matter (DM). The low-momentum scattering of such light DM is sensitive to the many-body structure of superfluid $^3$He, making it necessary to account for the collective behaviour of the medium. We present a first-principles calculation of the DM scattering rate in this inherently quantum many-body medium for both spin-independent and spin-dependent interactions. The leading consequence of this many-body behaviour is a kinematic enhancement of the scattering rate arising from the Fermi-Dirac statistics of the ultra-low-temperature target. Accounting for this extends the projected reach to lower DM masses for both spin-independent and spin-dependent interactions under the benchmark detector assumptions considered here. The resulting sensitivity improvement becomes increasingly pronounced as the detector threshold drops into the sub-eV regime. It is negligible at the conventional readout threshold of approximately $31$ eV, but becomes significant at the $0.51$ eV benchmark threshold for SQUID-based readout. Specifically, at this threshold, incorporating the many-body response extends the higher-energy recoil tail and improves the projected low-mass reach by approximately $2.5\%$ relative to the single-atom approximation. This improvement becomes more pronounced at lower thresholds. Reaching a threshold of $5$ meV could further extend the sensitivity of QUEST-DMC into the realm of sub-MeV DM.

**Relevance:** 1
**Novelty:** 5

## 62. [Note on quantum optimal measurements of CMB spectral distortions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.17552) <a id="link62"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2611.17552

**Authors:** Roshni Singh, Jacques Delabrouille, Daniel Carney

**Abstract:** As quantum sensing techniques continue to push the frontier of detection of ultra-weak signals, one may wonder if a sophisticated quantum strategy could be used to enable measurement of spectral distortions in the cosmic microwave background. Using quantum Fisher information techniques, we prove that the optimal strategy is simple frequency-binned intensity measurement. This proof strategy also enables us to provide numerical estimates for the information-theoretic minimum observation time required to observe the $\mu$- and $y$-distortions expected from $\Lambda$CDM cosmology. As case studies, we compare these results to the PIXIE and SPECTER mission concepts, and show that they are within a few orders of magnitude of this fundamental limit.

**Relevance:** 1
**Novelty:** 5

## 63. [WISE-VAULT: Decade-long Mid-Infrared Variability of WISE AGN](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.17996) <a id="link63"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2611.17996

**Authors:** Suvendu Rakshit

**Abstract:** Mid-infrared (MIR) variability provides a powerful probe of accretion processes and dust reprocessing in active galactic nuclei (AGNs), as it is less affected by extinction than optical flux. Using multi-epoch photometry from \textit{WISE} and NEOWISE spanning over a decade, we investigate the MIR variability of $\sim$4.5 million color-selected AGNs. Variability is quantified using maximum--minimum amplitudes ($\Delta W1$), excess variance, reduced $\chi^2$, and structure function (SF) analysis over timescales of 0.5--10 years. We identify 632,939 variable AGNs, including 54,897 sources that exhibit large-amplitude variability ($\Delta W1 > 0.5$ mag) and 624 with extreme intrinsic variability ($A_{\rm var} > 0.5$ mag). A subset of these sources shows flare-like behavior, identifying them as candidate changing-look AGNs or tidal disruption events that warrant further spectroscopic and multi-wavelength follow-up. The SF analysis shows radio-detected AGNs exhibit the highest variability amplitudes, followed by X-ray detected and the overall AGN sample. The variability amplitude is strongly anti-correlated with the Eddington ratio, indicating enhanced variability in lower accretion rate systems. A weak positive dependence on radio-loudness further suggests a contribution from non-thermal emission in radio sources. These results demonstrate that MIR variability provides a robust diagnostic of accretion physics and an efficient means of identifying extreme and rapidly evolving AGN phenomena. The full catalog of variable AGNs is made publicly available.

**Relevance:** 1
**Novelty:** 5

## 64. [Parity conservation of the isotropic galaxy 4PCF in redshift space](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.18515) <a id="link64"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2611.18515

**Authors:** Chanasorn Kongprachaya, Nicola Bartolo, Daniele Bertacca, Sabino Matarrese

**Abstract:** Recent observational investigation of cosmological Parity Violation (PV) through the galaxy Four-point Correlation Function (4PCF) has motivated a careful theoretical examination of potential systematic contributions. In this work, we demonstrate that parity-odd contributions to the isotropic 4PCF estimator induced by Redshift Space Distortion (RSD), up to the leading order $\mathcal{H}/k$ of general relativistic corrections, vanish upon averaging over all the Line-of-Sight (LOS). We analyse the perturbative relativistic galaxy number density, combining the biased tracer expansion and the dark matter gravitational dynamics within the framework of Standard Eulerian Perturbation Theory (EPT) to third order in the density perturbation. Building on prior studies of the parity-odd mode of the trispectrum (the Fourier-space counterpart of the 4PCF), we extend existing results in three directions: incorporating the wide-angle effect by adopting a local LOS definition for each galaxy in the 4PCF, accounting for tidal bias in the galaxy population, and generalising the assessment to an arbitrary cosmological background. Expanding the 4PCF in spherical harmonics, we show that angular momentum selection rules, encoded in the Wigner 3-j symbols, enforce that only parity-even contributions survive the integration over all LOS orientations, both in the plane-parallel approximation and in the full wide-angle case. This result is crucial for large-scale statistical analyses using isotropic estimators. It supports interpreting possible PV detections as a signal not due to a systematic RSD artefact. We conclude with a discussion of the caveats of our results and identify potential sources of PV.

**Relevance:** 1
**Novelty:** 5

## 65. [Characterization of the GIGAPYX-4600 CMOS visible image sensor in the context of high precision astrometry](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.18366) <a id="link65"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2611.18366

**Authors:** Manon Lizzana (IPAG, CNES), Hugo Rousset (IPAG), Sebastien Soler (IPAG), Fabrice Pancher (IPAG), Eric Thi{é}baut (CRAL), Fabien Malbet (IPAG)

**Abstract:** To evaluate the potential of detectors for next-generation space astrometry missions such as the HWO and Theia, an extensive analysis of the performance of the GIGAPYX-4600 46-megapixel CMOS detector from Pyxalis was conducted at IPAG. The 5456x8320-pixel detector, featuring a 4.4-micron pitch and back-side illumination, constitutes a matrix of 24x36 mm. It has a measured gain of 13.3 electrons per ADU in low-gain mode. The saturation level of the detector in low-gain-mode is estimated to be approximately 52 000 electrons. This sensor demonstrates linearity errors of 0.90%, pixel response non-uniformity of less than 1.3%, and readout and quantization noise of 12 electrons in low-gain mode. Dark current was measured at 35 electrons per second at 35{\textdegree}C and inter-pixel capacitance remained under 3%. We demonstrated that inter-pixel capacitance effects can be effectively mitigated using spatially varying kernel correction algorithms. Radiation tests confirmed its robustness against protons and heavy ions, and no latch-up was observed. These measurements agree with the manufacturer figures. These characterization results validate the GIGAPYX-4600 as a strong candidate for high-precision astrometry. Further improvements are currently being developed for space implementation.

**Relevance:** 1
**Novelty:** 5

## 66. [Periodic Modulation of Linear Polarization Fraction in Repeating FRB 20240114A](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.17805) <a id="link66"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2611.17805

**Authors:** Fang Sun, Yong-Feng Huang, Chen Du, Chen Deng, Pei Wang, Lun-Hua Shang, Chen-Ran Hu, Xiao-Fei Dong, Jin-Jun Geng, Ze-Cheng Zou, Nurimangul Nurmamat, Orkash Amat, Jing-Peng Lyu, Yun-Chuan Zhang

**Abstract:** Long-term polarization changes in repeating fast radio bursts (FRBs) can probe their emission geometry and propagation environment. We searched for periodic variation in the degree of linear polarization (DOL) of FRB~20240114A using the polarization catalog from the Five-hundred-meter Aperture Spherical radio Telescope. The sample contains 6,099 bursts detected on 95 observing days over $\sim 488$ days. The daily mean DOL series was analyzed using phase distance correlation and Lomb--Scargle periodograms over trial periods of 10--240 days. The strongest peaks occur at $119.91^{+2.67}_{-2.04}$ and $122.31^{+2.63}_{-2.82}$ days, respectively. The quoted ranges correspond to the period intervals above the global $3\sigma$ thresholds. Global false-alarm probabilities were estimated from \(10^7\) random permutations of the daily values among the fixed observing dates, with the full period search repeated for each permutation. The resulting probabilities are \(9.98\times10^{-4}\) and \(8.95\times10^{-4}\), corresponding to equivalent Gaussian significances of \(3.09\sigma\) and \(3.12\sigma\), respectively. As a supplementary check, the same analysis using daily median DOL yielded similar peaks near 120 days. A descriptive sinusoidal fit to all 95 daily mean DOL measurements at a fixed period of 119.91 days has a semi-amplitude of 3.23\%. Possible explanations include precession-driven changes in viewing geometry and variations in propagation conditions within a binary system.

**Relevance:** 1
**Novelty:** 5

## 67. [Zeeman-Doppler imaging of V889 Her: Surface spots and magnetic field topology 2017 - 2023](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.18310) <a id="link67"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2611.18310

**Authors:** Thomas Hackman, Jyri J. Lehtinen, Oleg Kochukhov, Pilvi Pirttikangas, Ilya Ilyin, Aandrás Haris, Maarit J. Korpi-Lagg, Sven Wedemeyer, Mikko Tuomi

**Abstract:** The surface magnetic field of the young solar analogue V889 Her is studied using Zeeman-Doppler imaging (ZDI). This well-studied star has previously been reported to have an exceptionally high differential rotation and show abrupt changes in the surface magnetic field and spot configuration. Our aim is to study the evolution of the magnetic field topology in V889 Her. This will contribute to understanding dynamo action in young solar-type stars. We also want to constrain the surface differential rotation curve of the target star.Methods. We employ the ZDI method on observations collected with the SOFIN spectropolarimeter mounted at the Nordic Optical Telescope. We test differential rotation parameters previously reported in studies of the star. We observe significant changes in the radial field near the rotational pole throughout the whole span of the observations. Specifically, the polarity of the radial field is reversed around 2021. The large-scale magnetic field also contains a persistent strong azimuthal component at high latitudes. We detect abrupt changes in the spot configuration in June 2018 on the time scale of a week. The observations favour a solar type differential rotation. The different rotation parameters influence the brightness map more than the magnetic field topology. We cannot link the observed changes in the magnetic field topology with the evolution of the long-term spot activity. The abrupt changes observed in 2018 confirm previous reports that the surface magnetic field and spot configuration may undergo rapid evolution on short time scales. This complicates the estimation of the surface differential rotation.

**Relevance:** 1
**Novelty:** 5

## 68. [Evidence for a Fast, Long-Period, Hot Population of Accreting Neutron Stars](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.17972) <a id="link68"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2611.17972

**Authors:** A. Patruno, M. A. Ferrer Martínez, B. Haskell, Y. Cavecchi

**Abstract:** Context. Accreting neutron stars in low-mass X-ray binaries allow us to probe the accretion histories that produce recycled millisecond pulsars. Aims. We expand the analysis of the spin distribution with an enlarged sample of 39 accreting neutron stars and investigate whether the high-frequency objects preferentially occur in binaries with longer orbital periods and have higher quiescent surface temperatures. Methods. We compare several empirical models for the spin distribution. We use stochastic drift-diffusion estimates and simple braking models to determine whether short-timescale torque fluctuations or secular spin-down can account for the observed population widths. Results. The best statistical description of the spin distribution consists of a broad low-frequency component and a narrow high-frequency concentration centred at ~566 Hz with a width of ~32 Hz. Fast rotators preferentially occupy longer orbital-period binaries, with possibly higher neutron star temperatures. Short-timescale stochastic accretion torque fluctuations cannot produce the observed spin-frequency dispersion in either the fast or the slow component, even under generous assumptions. We show that a non-linear deterministic braking mechanism can instead shift and compress a spin distribution, but the spin data alone do not identify the braking mechanism. Conclusions. The population widths appear to reflect differences in long-term torque conditions and/or evolutionary histories rather than short-timescale torque noise. If the fast systems are close to spin equilibrium, their narrow distribution requires the combination of physical parameters setting the equilibrium frequency to vary relatively little from source to source.Any model that explains the spin distribution should therefore also explain why the fast systems tend to have longer orbital periods and, possibly, higher temperatures.

**Relevance:** 1
**Novelty:** 5

## 69. [Quantum Efficiency Measurements of a Low-Cost, Commercial CMOS Sensor in the Soft X-ray Bandpass](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.18478) <a id="link69"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2611.18478

**Authors:** Casey T. DeRoo, Mallory Weber, Carson Bantz, Cole Primeaux, Hannah Gulick, Colin M. Packard, Philip Kaaret

**Abstract:** Complementary metal-oxide semiconductor (CMOS) image sensors offer distinct advantages as imaging detectors for space-based X-ray observatories, being low-power, radiation-resistant, and operable at room temperature. Previous characterizations of the low-cost, commercially-available IMX290LLR-C back-illuminated CMOS sensor from Sony Semiconductor Solutions have found Fano-limited X-ray spectral resolutions while operated at room temperature and a radiation tolerance compatible with use in low Earth orbit. Here, we report the quantum efficiency of the IMX290LLR-C as measured across the soft X-ray band (200 - 1000 eV) using Beamline 6.3.2 of the Advanced Light Source at Lawrence Berkeley National Laboratory. We find a quantum efficiency of roughly 10 -- 40\% over this band, and fit these measurements to a quantum efficiency model constrained by these measurements and microscopy of the sensor layers. These measurements and the derived model informs this sensor's application within low-cost X-ray observatories, as well as motivates the development of commercial X-ray CMOS sensors without microlenses and deeper Si depletion layers.

## 70. [Numerical experiments on sunspot penumbral flows](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.17946) <a id="link70"></a>

**Abstract:** We present 3D radiative magnetohydrodynamic simulations of sunspots with a self-consistently generated penumbra. We analyze the impact of resolution, surrounding opposite-polarity fields and subsurface large-scale flows on the structure and stability of the simulated penumbra. Extended penumbral filaments are obtained for a subset of the simulations. These were run at 25 km (15 km) horizontal (vertical) resolution and with initial conditions favoring fields with strong horizontal components in the penumbral region. The surface velocities in these simulations are consistent with observations of Evershed flow. The main driving mechanism remains the same as illustrated previously in Rempel (2012). The most successful simulations produce extended penumbrae using a potential magnetic field top boundary, in contrast to previous simulations of extended penumbra with an artificial enhancement of horizontal magnetic field at the top boundary.

## 71. [Holographically motivated Hubble-running dark energy: Phantom-divide crossing in VCDM and observational constraints](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.17374) <a id="link71"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2611.17374

**Authors:** Antonio De Felice, Burin Gumjudpai, Shinji Tsujikawa, Jack Zidich

**Abstract:** Motivated by power-law dependences on the expansion scale in holographic dark-energy constructions, we study the Hubble-running equation of state $w_{\rm DE}=w_0(H/H_0)^q$ (HoloEoS), where $H$ is the Hubble expansion rate, $H_0$ and $w_0$ are the present values of $H$ and $w_{\rm DE}$, and $q$ is a constant dimensionless exponent controlling the running. We embed this history in VCDM, a type-II minimally modified gravity theory that propagates only the two tensor modes of general relativity. For regular expanding histories with standard matter, we reconstruct the potential $V(\phi)$ of its nondynamical auxiliary scalar field, providing a Lagrangian description of both the homogeneous background and cosmological perturbations. This framework allows HoloEoS to realize a smooth late-time crossing of the phantom divide $w_{\rm DE}=-1$. We confront this VCDM realization with SPT, Planck, and ACT CMB data, updated DESI DR2 distances, and optionally DESY5 supernovae with Dovekie recalibration, comparing it with $\Lambda$CDM and the Chevallier--Polarski--Linder parameterization embedded in the same gravitational framework (CPL-VCDM). For the full data combination, the posterior medians and central $68\%$ credible intervals are $w_0=-0.881^{+0.040}_{-0.038}$ and $q=0.453^{+0.143}_{-0.133}$, with a crossing redshift $z_\times=0.476^{+0.106}_{-0.081}$ conditional on $0<6$. For this data combination, physical-likelihood optimization by Metropolis--Hastings (MH) simulated annealing gives Akaike information criterion differences of $\Delta\mathrm{AIC}\simeq-3.40$ for HoloEoS and $-4.10$ for CPL-VCDM relative to $\Lambda$CDM. Thus, a Hubble-scale-motivated dark-energy history realized within a specified gravitational theory achieves fitting performance comparable to CPL-VCDM while naturally allowing phantom-divide crossing.

## 72. [Axion quintessence with a negative cosmological constant: Bayesian constraints from current cosmological data](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.17725) <a id="link72"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2611.17725

**Authors:** Masroor C. Pookkillath, Gary Shiu, Shinji Tsujikawa

**Abstract:** We constrain canonical axion quintessence with a negative cosmological constant using DES Year 5 supernovae, DESI DR2 BAO, compressed CMB information, and geometry-corrected Gold-2017 growth data. We solve the background and linear-growth equations and sample with PolyChord. Our joint prior is logarithmic in the decay constant over $0.005\leq f/M_{\rm Pl}\leq10$ and uniform in the initial angle $\theta_i=\phi_i/f\in[0,\pi]$, conditioned on $\pi-\theta_i\geq H_{\rm inf}/(2\pi f)$ with $H_{\rm inf}=10^{13}$ GeV and reduced Planck mass $M_{\rm Pl}$. The broad-prior model favors recent thawing, with median $w_{{\rm DE},0}=-0.865^{+0.068}_{-0.053}$, $m_\phi\in[0.91,4.39]\times10^{-33}$ eV, and $f/M_{\rm Pl}\in[0.589,4.899]$ (central 68\% credible intervals). Bayes factors relative to flat $\Lambda$CDM are 0.70 and 30.1 for the normalized fluctuation-conditioned prior (Convention I) and its further conditioning on numerical acceptance (Convention II). Restricting $f\leq M_{\rm Pl}$ gives $f/M_{\rm Pl}\in[0.262,0.822]$ at 68\% credibility and Bayes factors 0.233 and 32.4. Relative to the zero-vacuum axion model, the broad-prior Bayes factors are 0.620 and 1.65, providing no compelling evidence for a negative cosmological constant. Local likelihood optimization gives an Akaike information criterion difference $\Delta{\rm AIC}\simeq-10.87$ for the zero-vacuum model relative to flat $\Lambda$CDM. For this model, assuming that the instanton generating $m_\phi^2f^2=\Lambda_{\rm UV}^4e^{-S_{\rm inst}}$ satisfies the axionic Weak Gravity Conjecture, the one-sided 95\% posterior lower limit on the minimum compatible coefficient $c_{\min}$ rises from 37 to 55 as the ultraviolet scale $\Lambda_{\rm UV}$ increases from $10^{-10}M_{\rm Pl}$ to $M_{\rm Pl}$, in tension with an order-one coefficient under these assumptions.

## 73. [Density fluctuations in pseudo-streamer and helmet streamer: Generation, propagation and comparison with Metis observations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.18326) <a id="link73"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2611.18326

**Authors:** Anmol Kumar, Simon Daley-Yates, Thomas A. Howson, Paolo Pagano, Ineke De Moortel, Vincenzo Andretta

**Abstract:** Recent white-light observations from the Metis coronagraph on board Solar Orbiter have shown the presence of propagating density fluctuations with a period of $\sim5$ minutes, particularly in the observationally bright regions of helmet streamers and pseudo-streamers. These density fluctuations have been shown to originate from magnetohydrodynamic wave modes -- slow and fast modes can directly compress the plasma, whereas Alfv\'en waves generate density fluctuations via a non-linear coupling with the slow and fast modes. In general, in low plasma $\beta$ regions, slow modes contribute more towards the generation of density fluctuations, whereas in high $\beta$ regions, there are significant contributions from both slow and fast waves. In this work, we investigate the propagation of (non-linear) Alfv\'en waves and the associated density fluctuations using a 2.5D axisymmetric global solar wind model that includes two distinct magnetic field topologies -- a helmet streamer and a pseudo-streamer. Key differences in these magnetic configurations give rise to differences in the wave behaviour due to, for example, the presence of a current sheet in the helmet streamer and an X-type null point in the pseudo-streamer, and the structure of the $\beta = 1$ layer in both magnetic field configurations. This leads to the possible creation of prominent interference patterns along the helmet streamer axis, which do not appear along the pseudo-streamer axis. Unlike in the streamer region, we also find that along the pseudo-streamer axis, the contribution to the density fluctuations is due to the fast waves only, despite being in a low $\beta$ plasma.

## 74. [Magnetic kink instability of jet-wind outflows. I. Static column configuration](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.18268) <a id="link74"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2611.18268

**Authors:** Krishna Kumar, Serguei S. Komissarov

**Abstract:** We describe the results of computer simulations investigating the stability of an axisymmetric static equilibrium combining a highly magnetised ($\sigma=10^3$) central column and a mildly ($\sigma=0.1$) to weakly ($\sigma=0.001$) magnetised outer shell with a helical and mostly force-free magnetic field of constant pitch. The measured linear growth rates of the instability are in agreement with the maximum growth rate for a uniform density column with a force-free magnetic field of constant pitch obtained by Appl et al. (2000), being only a little bit higher. At the non-linear phase, the interface separating the central column and the outer shell develops the characteristic corkscrew shape, with the space inside and between the coils of the corkscrew filled by the shell plasma. In high-resolution runs, a secondary instability is seen as well. It has the form of helical protuberances of low-$\sigma$ plasma intruding inside the corkscrew coils. These appear to be aligned with the local magnetic field lines. We tentatively identify the secondary instability as the magnetic Rayleigh-Taylor instability. Overall, the results indicate that the current-driven kink instability may provide the baryon-loading of AGN jets suggested by the observations.

## 75. [Extended Bayesian Estimation for In-flight Calibration of Space-based Instruments](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.18241) <a id="link75"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2611.18241

**Authors:** Soham Das, Yang Chen, David A. van Dyk, Herman L. Marshall, Vinay L. Kashyap, Michael Smith

**Abstract:** The ongoing calibration of space-based instruments is essential to ensure accurate measurements of physical quantities such as photon flux. The goal is to obtain a unified photon-flux measurement of a single astronomical source using data obtained with multiple instruments. Calibration involves in-flight adjustments to address discrepancies arising from instrument-specific variations. Taking a statistical approach, we extend a previously proposed additive log-Normal hierarchical model with half-variance correction based on a multiplicative physical model for photon counts. The newly proposed Bayesian hierarchical model can leverage domain-specific priors to account for correlated passbands and integrate information across different instruments, sources, and passbands effectively. The model is fitted using Markov chain Monte Carlo and can provide uncertainty quantification for instrument- and source-specific parameter estimation. Moreover, we obtain valid inferences for other structural parameters of the physics model. We use data derived from Chandra and XMM-Newton X-ray observatories to illustrate the proposed inference procedure.

**Abstract:** In the search for technosignatures, it can be useful to narrow down the cosmic haystack by prioritizing time-coordinate ``Schelling Points'' at which a beacon may be more likely to be transmitted. One such Schelling Point is the Anti-Solar Point (ASP) the place and moment at which Earth would lie directly between a hypothetical transmitter in the Earth Transit Zone (i.e., ecliptic) and the Sun. In this work, we describe a campaign to survey the ASP for radio technosignatures using the Allen Telescope Array, report the results of three initial nights of data from a pilot study in Summer 2023, and discuss the future of the campaign. In the pilot study, we find no sky-localized narrowband 1-2.4 GHz technosignatures within an RA range of approximately 20.266-21.312 hrs during the ASP, setting an upper limit flux density threshold of 115-936 Jy.

## 77. [Unsupervised Machine Learning Classification of FRB 20121102A across Distinct Active Episodes: Implications for FRB Emission Physics and Source Environments](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.18056) <a id="link77"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2611.18056

**Authors:** Xiao Li, En-Wei Liang

**Abstract:** Fast radio bursts (FRBs) are millisecond-duration radio transients of extragalactic origin. Identifying distinct burst populations and investigating their variations across active episodes provide important insights into the physical origins of repeating FRBs. In this paper, we apply an unsupervised machine learning approach to bursts from three active episodes of FRB 20121102A observed by the Five-hundred-metre Aperture Spherical Telescope (FAST). Our analysis reveals four distinct clusters primarily distinguished by their spectral properties. Notably, Cluster 1 exhibits bimodal-like distributions in central frequency and bandwidth and contains nearly all high-energy bursts, with a mean energy about an order of magnitude higher than those of the other clusters. It also occupies a distinct region in the width--fluence parameter space. These results suggest that this cluster may represent a distinct burst population potentially associated with different emission physics. Furthermore, the fractions of different burst clusters vary across active episodes. Cluster 1 accounts for about 27\% of the bursts in 2019, compared with about 8\% in 2022 and 2023. Cluster 2, with higher central frequencies and narrower bandwidths, has the largest fraction in 2019 and 2022, whereas Cluster 3, with broader bandwidths and intermediate central frequencies, has the largest fraction in 2023. These variations may indicate changes in the physical conditions of the emission regions or the local propagation environment across active episodes.

## 78. [Soft Excess Variability and a Test of X-ray Reprocessing in AGN Mrk 359 and NGC 4051 using NICER and TESS](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.17439) <a id="link78"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2611.17439

**Authors:** Tejas Sewak, Michael M. Fausnaugh, Edward M. Cackett, Javier A. García, Keith Horne, Rahul Jayaraman, Viraja C. Khatu, Ethan Partington, Raj Prince

**Abstract:** Continuum reverberation mapping of AGN accretion disks assumes that X-ray reprocessing drives the UV/optical variations on short timescales. This reprocessing should also produce an X-ray reflection spectrum, which could be the soft excess observed in many Type 1 AGN. Consequently, if the soft excess traces the reflected emission, the soft excess and optical light curves should be correlated because their variability is driven by the same illuminating X-ray continuum. We present results from a cross-correlation study using simultaneous NICER and TESS observations of $\sim$$30\mathrm{~days}$ for two AGN: Mrk 359 and NGC 4051. We performed time-resolved spectroscopic fits to the X-ray data using a model with power-law and soft-excess components. We find no strong correlation between the optical and soft excess light curves, with correlation coefficients $R(\tau)$ $\sim$0.5 for both sources. The total X-ray light curve also shows a similarly poor correlation with the optical variations. However, we find a stronger correlation between the primary X-ray flux and the soft excess flux for both sources. The lack of correlation between the primary X-rays and soft-excess with the optical variations implies that simple linear X-ray reprocessing is not the dominant process driving the optical variability. Either an intervening reprocessor between the inner X-ray region and outer disc is present that dilutes the X-ray-optical correlation, or the optical variability is driven by other mechanisms such as extreme-UV reprocessing or intrinsic disk variability.

## 79. [A helium-rich mass-donor in a 3.5-hr low mass X-ray binary](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.18226) <a id="link79"></a>

**Authors:** Duncan K Galloway (Monash University, Australia), Ryosuke Hirai (RIKEN, Japan), Adelle J. Goodwin (ICRAR Curtin, Australia)

**Abstract:** Binary systems in which a neutron star accretes material from a low-mass stellar companion arise from evolutionary processes that depend sensitively on the progenitor properties. It is usually assumed that the accreted material has approximately solar composition, unless the orbital period is $\lesssim80$ min, indicating an "ultracompact" system with a hydrogen deficient donor. However, few constraints on the donor composition are available. Here we apply a Bayesian model-comparison framework, BEANSP, to a sample of 21 thermonuclear bursts from the accretion-powered millisecond pulsar, IGR J17511-3057, to constrain the fuel composition as well as the system parameters. Our analysis strongly indicates an extremely hydrogen- and CNO-deficient fuel composition, with $X \approx 0.03$ and $Z_\rm{CNO}\approx 4 \times 10^{-4}$, unexpected for this system with its relatively long 3.5 hr orbital period. We explore the possible circumstances under which such an object can evolve, and discuss the implications for its history also in light of the three transient outbursts that have been observed to date. Such analyses are finding an unexpectedly wide range in donor compositions, that may help to explain the remarkable diversity in the burst properties of the known burst sources

## 80. [Squeezing Drives Southward Magnetic Field Enhancement During 10 May 2024 CME-CME Interaction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.17807) <a id="link80"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2611.17807

**Authors:** Ziwei Wang, Liping Yang, Xueshang Feng, Jiansen He, Hui Tian, Fang Shen, Zhenyong Hou, Rui Wang, Xiao Zhang, Ming Xiong

**Abstract:** The southward magnetic field carried by coronal mass ejections (CMEs) is a key driver of space weather events, yet how interacting CMEs amplify this field remains debated. The 10 May 2024 event, which produced the strongest geomagnetic storm since 2003, offers an ideal testbed for this question. Combining multi-spacecraft observations with high-resolution controlled numerical experiments, we track the CMEs continuously from their origin near the solar surface to beyond Earth, and find that inherent squeezing during CME-CME interactions generates the sustained enhancement of the southward field, exceeding 60 nT in magnitude near Earth. First driven by the dynamic pressure of the trailing CME and later maintained by magnetic and thermal pressures at the CME-CME interface, squeezing transforms the preceding CME from a bulky cloud into a flattened "pancake" structure, prevents expansion-induced field decay, and maintains an amplified southward field over long distances. The strength of this enhancement scales with the intensity of the squeezing. Our findings establish a direct link between CME-CME interaction and strong southward fields, laying a physics-based foundation for understanding and forecasting the geoeffectiveness of interacting CMEs.

## 81. [Gravitational production of dark QED by the trace anomaly](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.18337) <a id="link81"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2611.18337

**Authors:** Simon Cléry, Aliaksei Kachanovich, Michel HG Tytgat

**Abstract:** We revisit the production of fermionic dark matter $\chi$ through the graviton portal, sourced by inflaton annihilation at the end of inflation. Because the production rate is helicity-suppressed, $\propto m_\chi^2$, the dark fermion must be very heavy to account for the observed relic abundance, $m_\chi \gtrsim 10^{10}$ GeV, with the exact bound set by the reheating temperature $T_{\rm RH}$. Here we examine how interactions within the dark sector could modify this result. As a toy model, we consider dark QED, in which $\chi$ couples to a light dark photon. We first show that, as a consequence of classical conformal invariance, bremsstrahlung of a dark photon does not lift the helicity suppression for light fermions. We then show that the trace anomaly of dark QED, which affects the coupling of the graviton to dark photons, can produce light dark fermions via a Dalitz process. This allows to extend the fermionic dark mass range possibly down to $m_\chi \sim 10^7$~GeV, depending on $T_{\rm RH}$ and the dark QED coupling. We also determine under which conditions the hidden sector reaches thermal equilibrium and derive a lower bound on the temperature ratio at reheating $(T'/T)_{\rm RH}$ as function of the dark QED coupling and the reheating temperature.

## 82. [Multimessenger Exoplanets: Combining Infrared Excess and Astrometry with Gravitational-wave Detections of Double White Dwarf Exoplanets](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.17572) <a id="link82"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2611.17572

**Authors:** Casey McGrath, Mary Anne Limbach, Scott C. Noble, Vincent Kofman

**Abstract:** Multimessenger astrophysics provides a powerful framework for characterizing astrophysical systems through multiple, independent signals rather than relying solely on electromagnetic (EM) observations. While most prior multimessenger studies have focused on the properties of ultracompact objects, recent work has shown that gravitational waves (GWs) detectable with the upcoming Laser Interferometer Space Antenna (LISA) observatory can also be used to detect exoplanets orbiting stellar remnants. In particular, this includes circumbinary planets around tight double white dwarf (DWD) systems. This population is unique: no such planets are currently known around DWDs, and only a handful of circumbinary planets around main-sequence stars and planets orbiting white dwarfs (WDs) have been identified. Independent EM confirmation will be essential, especially for the first GW-detected exoplanets, to validate this method. While EM techniques are unlikely to efficiently survey large populations of DWDs, GW detection followed by targeted EM confirmation provides one pathway for identifying and characterizing these systems. Using a representative case, the known DWD ZTF J2243+5242 scaled to a fiducial distance of 150 pc, we compute the detectability of circumbinary exoplanets via LISA GW observations. We then demonstrate two complementary EM techniques--JWST infrared (IR) excess and Gaia or Habitable Worlds Observatory astrometry--that could independently confirm a GW-detected planet in this fiducial system. We present the first exploration of IR excess around DWDs as a viable exoplanet detection method, extending its established use for solitary WDs. Both EM techniques overlap with regions of parameter space where LISA is sensitive to DWD exoplanets, enabling robust confirmation and characterization.

**ArXiv ID:** 2611.17833

**Authors:** Huan Zhou, Kai Liao, Zhengxiang Li, Cheng-Gang Shao

**Abstract:** Fast radio bursts (FRBs) are millisecond duration coherent radio transients, some of whose sources repeat, but the nature and active lifetimes of their engines remain uncertain. Strong lensing by galaxy clusters provides a physical lever on this lifetime problem: a short campaign can sample emission epochs of a repeating FRB separated by years, and distinct bursts in different lensed images, given a known relative lens delay, set a lower bound on its active lifetime, whereas delayed copies of a single burst only encode the lens delay. Based on the cluster-lens model of MACS J0717.5+3745, we quantify the accessible range of emission epochs for finite activity episodes, observing windows, and discovery selection. For a source-frame episode of duration \(100~{\rm yr}\) at \(z_s=2\) and a source-frame rate of \(0.1\,{\rm h}_{\rm src}^{-1}\), a five year campaign conditional on discovery yields a \(95\%\) gain threshold of \(m_{95}\simeq25.6\), corresponding to a source-frame span of \(L_{95}\simeq42.6~{\rm yr}\). At the population level, combining distinct burst associations with same burst echoes provides a way to test specified activity durations with a finite lens catalogue, e.g., activity durations differing by an order of magnitude can be distinguished with expected Bayes factor above $20$. More broadly, these lensed source frame epochs enable applications beyond activity-duration tests, e.g., linking burst activity to the evolution of the engine and its environment. Galaxy-cluster lensing therefore provides a promising future probe for accessing otherwise unobserved source history and for distinguishing between different activity duration models.

## 85. [KELP v1.0: an agent-ready Lagrangian particle tracker for unstructured meshes and HEALPix grids](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.18528) <a id="link85"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2611.18528

**Authors:** Ivan Kuznetsov, Alexey Androsov, Dmitrii Pantiukhin, Claudia Wekerle, Sergey Danilov, Thomas Jung, Nikolay Koldunov

**Abstract:** Lagrangian particle tracking follows virtual particles in the velocity fields of an ocean model to study transport pathways, connectivity and dispersion. Setting up such a study takes many technical steps, and this work is increasingly done with coding agents, programs in which a language model writes code, runs it and checks the result. We present KELP v1.0, the Kinematic Engine for Lagrangian Particles, a tracker for unstructured ocean-model meshes and HEALPix grids that is built to be operated by such agents. KELP computes two- and three-dimensional trajectories with established methods on triangular, quadrilateral and mixed meshes, with global FESOM2 and coastal FESOM-C meshes as examples, and on HEALPix, the equal-area grid on which kilometre-scale digital twins distribute their output. Documentation and short task instructions, called skills, tell an agent how to prepare releases and configurations, run calculations and process the trajectories. An agent prepared and ran all experiments of this paper with these skills, and most of the preparation was completed within hours. In a controlled evaluation, the skills raised the completeness of study files prepared by five language models from 75-93% to 88-100%. KELP reproduced the particle distributions of two other trackers on the same fields. Trajectories on HEALPix fields converged to those on the model mesh as the pixels were refined. After one year, the median distance between the two was 1.6 km at a pixel spacing of 25.5 km. HEALPix output can therefore be used for Lagrangian studies of ensemble statistics when the original mesh is not available. In a 63-year calculation with 7.92 million particles released in the Beaufort Gyre, transport from the gyre to Fram Strait took years to decades, and the run took 3 h 36 min on one 128-core node. A test tracked $10^{10}$ particles on a HEALPix mesh of 35.5 million wet surface nodes.

## 86. [Verifying the High-Energy Tail in Bi-Kappa Particle Loading: Three-Dimensional Tests, Velocity Bounds, and Finite Precision](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.18499) <a id="link86"></a>

**ArXiv ID:** 2611.18499

**Authors:** Keheng Zhu, Yuri A. Omelchenko

**Abstract:** The bi-Kappa distribution models anisotropic space-plasma populations with suprathermal power-law tails. Kinetic simulations represent such a population by computational particles, and a loader draws their velocities from the requested bi-Kappa distribution, which we call the target distribution. When $\kappa$ is close to 1/2, floating-point underflow can remove the largest draws, and a velocity bound changes the loaded distribution. Published Kappa loaders are usually verified by comparing histograms of the speed or of one velocity component with the analytic distribution. Such comparisons do not examine the direction of the velocity or whether the direction is independent of the speed. We build an anisotropic loader on an established Gamma-ratio construction and examine each of these problems. Near $\kappa=1/2$, evaluating the Gamma ratio on a logarithmic scale avoids the loss of draws to underflow in single and double precision down to $\kappa=0.5001$. When draws outside a bound on each velocity component are discarded and redrawn, a small rejected fraction can accompany a large change in the tail. At $\kappa=2$ and $\theta_\parallel=2\theta_\perp$, a bound at 20 times each component's thermal speed rejects a fraction $3.6\times10^{-4}$ of the draws and lowers the 99.9th-percentile speed by 11%. We test the loaded velocities through their direction and their normalized radius, the length of the velocity after each component is divided by $\sqrt{\kappa}$ times its thermal speed. The tests used nine values of $\kappa$ from 0.51 to 10, including $1/2<\kappa\le3/2$, where the second moments do not exist, with $\theta_\parallel=2\theta_\perp$ in double precision. At every $\kappa$, $\chi^2$ tests find the normalized radius out to its 99.999th percentile, the direction, and their joint distribution consistent with the target distribution.

## 87. [High-resolution Observations of Physical Properties of Dynamic Fibrils Around a Sunspot](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.17838) <a id="link87"></a>

**Authors:** Guotang Wu, Xiaoli Yan, Zhike Xue, Jincheng Wang, Zhe Xu, Liheng Yang, Yadan Duan, Yian Zhou, Xinsheng Zhang, Qifan Dong, Zongyin Wu

**Abstract:** Understanding the formation and evolution of dynamic fibrils (DFs) in active regions is essential for uncovering the physical processes operating in the highly structured and magnetized solar atmosphere. High-resolution chromospheric observations from the New Vacuum Solar Telescope (NVST) show DFs with periodic up-and-down motions. We carry out a statistical analysis of 176 DFs around a sunspot. The mean values of deceleration, maximum velocity, maximum length, and duration of the DFs are $120 \boldsymbol{\pm 43}~\mathrm{m~s^{-2}}$, $15.9 \boldsymbol{\pm 4.5}~\mathrm{km~s^{-1}}$, $1.13 \boldsymbol{\pm 0.48}~\mathrm{Mm}$, and $270 \boldsymbol{\pm 63}~\mathrm{s}$, respectively. Several pairs of parameters exhibit correlations, most notably a clear positive correlation between deceleration and maximum velocity. The consistency between the periodicities of DFs and running penumbral waves is first identified. We further find that DFs in regions with more vertical magnetic fields exhibit larger decelerations and shorter periods, whereas those in regions with more horizontal magnetic fields exhibit the opposite behavior. These results, together with the presence of running penumbral waves, support the interpretation that DFs are driven by slow magnetoacoustic waves. Furthermore, the multiwavelength signatures of DFs from the chromosphere to the corona imply a potential contribution to energy transport and heating in the solar atmosphere.

**Relevance:** 1
**Novelty:** 4
